## Current checkpoint — 2026-11-22, beta restart and restore regression

- Third beta swap (2026-11-22, ~20:50) installed the aligned build from `/tmp/matou-mac-release-v11` (contains `ba06bbf3`, `7b321659`, `403b235f`). The previous instance quit through a targeted quit Apple Event (no focus change, production `/Applications/码头.app` untouched), the old bundle was archived to `/tmp/matou-beta-backup-20261122-205007.zip`, and all four Developer ID signatures verified with `--require-release-signature` after installation. Desktop PID `66281` / Runtime PID `66290` run on `~/.matou-beta`. The real window (`/tmp/matou-restore-shots/02-beta-aligned-banner.png`) shows the three failed child cards with the strip only: actions on one row, wrapped title, ellipsized reason. The two older cards still display their previously recorded `provider resume exited with code 1` text until a retry re-derives it. The new beta uses bundle ID `com.matou.desktop.beta` (the previous two installs shared production's `com.matou.desktop`), so TCC/document permissions will be requested separately from production.
- Held-scrollbar wheel residual resolved as pacing, not loss (`/tmp/matou-scroll-settle/report.md`): native Release, the earlier Debug build and the frozen reference all started at row #1263 and all ended at #1277 (+14 lines) on every run; at 400 ms, 800 ms and 1 s after the last packet the difference is 0 lines. At 150 ms native is 1–3 lines behind and at 225 ms 0–1 behind. Measured arrival estimates: Release ≈24 ms slower than the reference, Debug ≈46 ms (three-run medians). Quartz delivered 28 packets, 27 with movement, identical sequence per run, total −154; the thumb moved 6 px in all three (Release's absolute position was already 2 px off before the wheel). No product change; the end state matches and the pacing gap is recorded.
- User decision (2026-11-22): the recovery-water animation is marked passed on the previously agreed "not visually indistinguishable" bar (geometry/position/size/colour/anchor/timing exact; rasterisation may differ), closing the zero-tolerance pixel gate. The recorded first-frame/physical-presentation measurements remain as data, not as open gates.
- User authorized replacing and reopening the macOS beta only. Installed signed build `22840512` at `/Applications/码头测试版.app`; old instance exited normally and old bundle was archived as `/tmp/matou-beta-backup-20261122-194126.zip`. Desktop PID `59880` and Runtime PID `59884` started on `~/.matou-beta`. All four Developer ID signatures passed strict verification. macOS ZIP SHA-256 `0a676dea55ca56e79acf4b9706b9c3efcdec8e8ec1ff00fc626b2ff2097a8cb7`. No production replacement or release.
- Post-restart macOS beta feedback (Image #43): three Claude child cards failed to restore. Read-only beta inspection found `SessionStart` recorded as the last hook on all three successful fork records; child transcripts were not found in the default Claude projects directory, while the parent transcript exists. The direct cause of the CLI error is an unavailable child conversation; why the child transcript is absent still needs an isolated reproduction. Native still paints the exited terminal below the failure banner; Electron `TerminalPane.tsx:481–484` excludes it for failed auto-restore providers. `matou-restore-regression-01` is investigating the missing-transcript path and correcting the confirmed UI difference in isolation. No real session binding, transcript or data has been repaired or replaced.
- Merged `ba06bbf3` (`4f3e64bf`) into `codex/native-parity`: a failed auto-restore now shows only the recovery strip, like Electron (`TerminalPane.tsx:481–484`). The card no longer paints the exited CLI terminal, and pointer, keyboard and focus restoration stop targeting it; the header history entry still opens the original conversation, and a recovered run reuses the same terminal. Narrow cards keep the title single-line and wrap the action group only when needed. Verified with `session_levels_probe --provider-restore`: red before the fix (`restore failure painted the CLI terminal`), green after, covering late failed frames, focus/input, history, recovery frame at 260/274 pt selected/unselected in both themes, plus the existing pending, timeout, deferred and start-failure cases; re-run on the merged tree. Still hidden-probe evidence only: no visible paired Electron/native window at matched widths yet.
- Root cause of the three stranded child cards, from isolated real-CLI reproduction (`/tmp/matou-wt-restore-regression/artifacts/native-parity/restore-regression/{causes,symptoms,tests}.md`): Claude Code 2.1.330 does not write a child transcript for `--resume parent --fork-session` when the child exits without any turn; a child with one completed turn persists and restores. Matou records fork success on the first valid `SessionStart`, so `succeeded` can precede any persisted child conversation. Read-only beta data agrees: all three cards have `lastHookEvent=SessionStart`, `succeeded` fork intents pointing at parent `c5273bbc…`, a readable parent transcript, and child-scoped `~/.claude/file-history` / `session-env` directories but no child `.jsonl` anywhere. Retrying cannot restore them; only an explicit rebuild from the parent could, and that is a product decision (the rebuilt card would carry the parent's current content, not the exact fork point).
- Exit-code masking is a separate verified defect: at 32 columns the CLI wraps the UUID across lines, and `provider_resume.rs` requires the contiguous expected ID, so two cards recorded `provider resume exited with code 1` instead of `provider session not found`. Native PTY delivers output before exit at 32/48/80/120 columns, so only the matcher needs fixing.
- Merged `7b321659` (`b4a88bc5`): resume-failure recognition now matches whitespace-free output for Claude, Antigravity and Codex, still scoped to the expected/bound identity, keeping the existing reason strings and exit fallbacks. Unit tests split the UUID and the background message at every position with newline and cursor-move sequences, keep mismatch and distance cases negative, and replay a real 32-column recording of the CLI error; red before, green after. In the merged tree the 12 resume unit tests and the two Claude restore process-contract tests pass, and `pnpm check:identifiers`/`git diff --check` are clean. The full runtime suite has two failing targets that fail identically on the clean baseline (`process_contract` 36 same-named cases, `worker_contract` `Operation not permitted`), attributed to the isolated environment, not this change. `cargo fmt --check` is not a usable gate with the current rustfmt: six diffs already exist on untouched baseline files.
- Merged `5128e0b3` (`c0f93990`): an empty forked Claude card no longer has to stay failed. When a restore fails with `provider session not found` and the card provably never received a prompt (fork intent succeeded, source is another Claude conversation, binding metadata has no observed prompt), the runtime relaunches the same binding with `--resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <child>` and keeps the same card and binding. Guards: parent and child IDs must be valid UUIDs and distinct (the CLI would append to the parent file if equal), the parent transcript must be found under the launch environment's `projects/`, an existing child transcript blocks the rebuild, and a failed rebuild is recorded with a distinguishable reason so neither the same run nor the next launch tries again. Already-parked cards rebuild through the existing 重试恢复 entry; startup only attempts cards that are actually activated. Evidence: 8 `provider_resume` unit tests, `process_contract::claude_fork_rebuild` (automatic, manual, parent absent, has prompt, existing child transcript, parent disappearing after preflight, child ID in use, silent exit), the core launch contract, and a real-CLI isolated smoke (`apps/native/target/rebuild-evidence/cli-w426lbsy/verification.json`) whose second start is exactly `--resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <child>`, renders the parent's two turns, receives a child reply, and leaves the parent `.jsonl` SHA-256 unchanged. Not verified: the real desktop window on the user's three cards, Windows, and the known content caveat that a rebuild carries the parent's current history, not the original fork point.
- Merged `c8c3c404` (`45fa134c`): the empty-fork rebuild now preflights both transcripts before launching, so an eligible card starts the CLI **once** with `--resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <child>` instead of running a doomed resume and then rebuilding. - Rebuild semantics measured on the real CLI 2.1.330 (`/tmp/matou-cli-rebuild-research/report.md`, isolated HOME/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, loopback-only upstream): `--resume <parent> --fork-session --session-id <child>` is accepted and reports the given child ID; after one child turn it writes `<child>.jsonl`, which then resumes normally. The rebuilt conversation uses the parent's **current** history — turns added to the parent after the original fork are included (inherited rows keep their `uuid`/`parentUuid`; only `sessionId` changes). A rebuild without any child turn writes nothing (repeatable, still unrestorable until a turn exists); with an existing child file the CLI refuses with `Session ID <id> is already in use.`; `--session-id` with `--resume` requires `--fork-session`; a missing parent exits 1 with `No conversation found with session ID: <parent>`. Danger recorded: if the child ID equals the parent ID, the CLI accepts and appends new turns to the parent file, so rebuild must never run with equal IDs. Parent file and `session-env` stayed byte-identical in every non-equal case.

- **Decision:** clean-exit backup is created only for an explicit parent shutdown request. Stdin EOF, decode failure and internal runtime errors are not eligible (frozen has no equivalent EOF backup entry); they still close the DB and release the lease. Backup must run on the owner thread after session teardown and before DB/lease destruction, never from `Drop`.

### N02bb / L04b — macOS delayed quit (implemented; isolated real evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Root-reviewed implementation: `applicationShouldTerminate:` added to the existing `GPUIApplicationDelegate` class at runtime (no vendor patch, no new dependency; `core-foundation =0.10.0` was already a desktop dependency). First request arms a worker and returns `NSTerminateLater`; the worker joins the supervisor, then replies exactly once through a shared `Waiting` gate using two delivery entries (main-queue `dispatch_async` and a CoreFoundation main-run-loop one-shot timer in modal/common modes) because GPUI's `cx.quit()` nests `terminate:` inside a main-queue callback. The begin closure sets `quitting`, hides the window, queues the final `PutGeometry` through `AsyncApp`, then calls `RuntimeBridge::shutdown_async` (new method: close admission + hand off the supervisor). `Drop` and non-Mac paths unchanged; hook/defer failures fall back to the previous synchronous quit.
- Executor's isolated evidence (distinct bundle ids/data per run, wrapper child keeps the real runtime alive): AppleScript quit window offscreen ~82 ms, main thread in `CFRunLoop`, app exits ~26 ms after the child; GPUI `cx.quit()` sample shows the nested `_shouldTerminate` loop and the final teardown; child exit code 19 still quits the app with status 0 and logs the error; repeated quit yields exactly 1 `Shutdown` and 1 AppKit `reply YES`; last geometry (focus 4c3a8a5e→3d4db7f3, anchor 320.67→10) reaches the runtime ~49 µs before `Shutdown`, and both the live DB and the clean-exit snapshot read it back with `quick_check=ok`. `terminate-01/REPORT.md` plus per-case timeline/sample/logs.
- Root independently reran Mac `cargo check --lib --bins`, desktop lib tests 10/10, fmt, identifiers and diff-check. Physical Cmd+Q/menu/Dock, supervisor-thread panic injection, and Windows execution remain unverified; GNU check still stops at a pre-existing terminal-crate build-script/PATH issue (MSVC check passes).

## N01 — selected native boundaries and first implementation release

The full existing product remains the frozen scope. Baseline evidence is completed per dependency before releasing the corresponding implementation node; unobserved GUI/performance scenarios remain open, not removed. The first released node is the native persistence foundation used by the first real Shell lifecycle, not a replacement UI or an acceptance milestone. Its own source behavior, on-disk schema and performance were measured by B04S. GUI/PTY node releases still require their applicable baseline.

- Rust workspace lives in apps/native. `core` has no GPUI, Node or browser dependency. Root owns workspace manifests, generated migration catalog/fixtures and shared interface decisions. Native GUI will be a separate GPUI client crate; the final shared runtime will be a Rust process preserving the existing client/runtime ownership and CLI control behavior. No temporary runtime integration is currently in use.
- The runtime owns domain commands, durable SQLite state and PTYs. Existing command/event envelopes and SQLite tables are retained. GUI entities own focus/layout/animation/selection, never write the DB or claim an operation succeeded before the runtime result.
- One runtime-owned serialized path mutates each database. Before a runnable runtime is introduced it must acquire the existing data-directory ownership contract; migrations/backup/recovery policy are implemented at that owner boundary. N02a accepts a caller-owned SQLite connection and does not open any user's path, acquire a runtime lease, or claim backup/recovery completeness.
- PTY output must be persisted and processed in sequence. Ghostty handles are created/destroyed on their owner thread; owned screen data is sent to GPUI. Feed/resize/sync expiry route terminal replies to the same live PTY; replay drops replies. Shell completed-command recording uses the existing OSC 633 E / OSC 133 D stream contract, not scraped screen text. D01 restoration occurs before new live output without a subsequent replay reset erasing it.
- Reuse candidates after read-only audit: old lab Ghostty C shim/wrapper, owned Cell/Screen and basic input/selection helpers. Do not import dual-engine selection, demo UI, JSON capture of entire output, old fixed dark palette or fixed 9x18 cell metrics. Missing cursor shape, soft-wrap metadata, full key/mouse modes and OSC effects remain explicit work; basic helpers do not satisfy their acceptance.

### N02a fixed implementation path and API

Port `storage/migration-runner.ts` and `storage/domain-transaction.ts` semantics directly. Generated `core/schema/migrations.json` is the complete 35-migration frozen source catalog with v2/legacy checksums; no hand-written replacement schema. `core/tests/fixtures/storage-reference.json` contains the 87 schema definitions and results actually emitted by reference source on captured Electron, including committed event rows, dedup row, replay, conflicting request and failed-mutation rollback.

Implement in `core/src/storage/{mod,migrations,transaction}.rs`, exported by `core/src/lib.rs`:

1. `configure_connection(&Connection) -> Result<()>`: existing WAL, foreign_keys ON, synchronous FULL, busy_timeout 5000, trusted_schema OFF; create only existing _runtime_meta table. Runtime generation is the future lease owner's responsibility.
2. `migrate(&mut Connection, applied_at: i64) -> Result<MigrationReport>`: ordered frozen migrations, reject newer schema/checksum mismatch, accept existing legacy checksums and update history names/checksums exactly as source. Each pending migration is an IMMEDIATE transaction. For foreignKeysOff, set OFF/legacy_alter_table ON outside transaction, verify foreign_key_check before commit, restore pragmas on success/error. Report applied versions/current version. Caller owns pre-migration backup and recovery UX, not this primitive.
3. `execute(&mut Connection, &CommandMetadata, committed_at: i64, mutation) -> Result<DomainCommit>`: source-compatible camelCase serde metadata/event/commit types; mutation receives transaction plus event vector and returns serde_json::Value. Dedup lookup first; identical request returns persisted response without invoking mutation; same ID/different hash errors. An IMMEDIATE transaction contains mutation, ordered event inserts and dedup response. Any failure rolls all three back. Preserve optional fields and defaults from source; no event bus, async framework or new retry layer.

Tests are cross-implementation contracts, not snapshots derived from the new Rust code: compare all schema objects with source fixture; compare transaction result/event/dedup fields; exercise replay without rerunning closure, request conflict, rollback, legacy/newer/corrupt migration history and close/reopen persistence. Only run this crate's tests and formatting during implementation. Root owns integrated validation and actual GUI acceptance later. Schema/transaction equality cannot be reported as native product delivery.

### N02f release — journal before live Shell

Root reviewed the complete frozen segment writer/reader, tail index, range index, compression policy/worker, and the actual PTY output/resize/exit call sites. Output publication and completed-command derivation occur only after journal append succeeds; resize and exit flush the 16ms/64Ki UTF-16-unit output batch first. Native implementation must preserve that order at the forthcoming runtime seam.

The new source exporter `tooling/native-parity/export-journal-contract.ts` ran the original source in captured Electron on disposable paths. `core/tests/fixtures/journal-reference.json` records eight frames (all six kinds, including split UTF-8 bytes), two original raw segments and gzip equivalents, five strict/torn/corrupt cases, three complete tail-index snapshots, compression candidate policy and source sidecars. Timestamps are captured so native record encoding can be compared byte for byte. Gzip decoder results are compared; compressor implementations are not expected to emit identical compressed bytes.

Root owns `journal/model.rs` (Frame/Record/error/constants), module exports, manifests and fixtures. N02f1 and N02f2 have disjoint files and a fixed API, allowing implementation against the established contract. Codec is a one-frame-at-a-time iterator with a 64MiB frame limit; no full-history vector is needed. Tail index retains the original 10,000-line/sparse/domain-cursor contract. Lifecycle uses the original 16MiB segments/256MiB raw hot budget, actual background gzip level6 (two workers), atomic index publication and the original healthy-suffix recovery policy. Index publication is coalesced off the append path; close/rotation waits for it. No node is yet accepted and neither has been integrated into a live PTY.

Windows directory-sync errors remain visible for ownership/segments/range/compression. Only the source tail-index path already ignores that platform's directory-sync error. No Windows filesystem execution has established a new policy. The first native product slice still requires real PTY, visible GPUI, restart restoration and applicable performance comparison.

### N02i–k — live sessions and completed-history integration

### N02o–q — real Shell domain state connected to startup

- The source graph/status exporter takes an online backup of a disposable frozen-source database and records 16 sequential operations. Native projection, work-status results, domain rows/events/receipts, same-status emission and replay all match. The exercised fields include mixed active/archived children, shared cwd, summary filtering, local failure, Git branch/detached/unavailable and window focus. Provider recovery, fork progress, managed worktree and detached-window branches are ported from the complete source projection but need their own capability-stage evidence before full acceptance.
- Ordinary-Shell derivation compares 22 work-status streams, seven cwd streams, seven summary inputs and one UTF16-tail case. The exporter executes the original trackers and extracts the unchanged three summary function bodies from the frozen runtime server (only TypeScript annotations removed); it never launches that server. Regex/URL/percent-decoding use pinned standard crates to retain ECMAScript whitespace/counting and WHATWG file URL behavior. The native string boundary represents a split lone surrogate as U+FFFD, matching the existing byte/SQLite conversion, without changing PTY bytes.
- `SavedOutput` contains a persisted frame and its collected completed blocks; `SavedExit` contains the persisted exit and its disposal origin. They are delivered to the owner before credit-limited display output/exit and never during journal replay. Source onOutput/onExit run independently of UI credit too. Existing real session tests now verify these same facts through the new internal representation: exact journal bytes/sequences, no saved callback on failed append, retry exactly once, 2.2MiB output and pre-ack exit, no duplicate after replay. The worker handshake exposes the actual PID before run insertion; failure to insert a run shuts that worker down.
- `ShellOwner` now requires the generation written by real writable startup. It interrupts previous-generation active runs with original transaction metadata before launching a new worker; records start/finish with PID, ordinal and exit status; emits committed domain ranges; and preserves active-work runs on shutdown so the next generation marks them interrupted. It does not label an interrupted command as a completed history block.
- Every worker envelope carries its run identity. This is required by the existing source's live-instance comparisons: late output/OS cwd results from a retired worker cannot mutate a replacement run. Native natural exit retains the last terminal state for display until relaunch/disposal. Failed shell launch creates no run record.
- Enter on a healthy, nonfrozen Shell emits one input-submission event before PTY write, allowing running-state feedback and the original 180ms/1050ms cwd captures. Captures use the same asynchronous macOS lsof/Linux proc query; the Windows source has no OS cwd query and continues to rely on OSC7. The central owner validates the captured directory and run identity before saving. Completed history is persisted before cwd/work-status derivation, summaries keep the original 32768-unit buffer/180ms timer/last-four-lines filters, and successful completion flushes its summary in the same event turn. Disabling restoration also disables new block collection, as in the source.
- Actual Mac owner tests start through `startup::open`, execute success/failure and a real zsh read prompt, answer it, cd into a subdirectory, inspect the persisted graph, exit7 and relaunch with a distinct PID/next ordinal. Restart test preserves only completed Chinese/emoji history, marks the interrupted old run correctly and confirms disabled collection does not append new blocks. An initial test-only prompt omitted zsh's %% escape; a later cwd assertion initially compared /var with macOS lsof's /private/var. The observed paths were recorded before changing the test to expect the actual OS canonical path. No production behavior was altered to hide either test issue.

Current boundary: these are actual PTYs, storage and owned terminal screens, not a visible native product. The GUI, standalone runtime process/IPC, preferences/application command surface, recovery actions and bounded clean-exit backup are still open. The next vertical step is formal GPUI wiring using this background owner; complete Mac/Windows visual, interaction and performance acceptance remain unproven. Main production app/data remain untouched.

### N02w–x — carousel motion, hover browsing and viewport persistence

- `Transition` implements the frozen width/lift cubic curves and CSS reversing shortening behavior. The reference exporter executes the unchanged frozen CSS in an isolated Chromium window, samples full expansion/collapse and two reversals, and stores browser timing/geometry in `carousel-motion-reference.json`. The native numerical contract passes all samples within Chromium's 1/64px layout quantization; this does not define a pixel-diff tolerance. Repeated terminal paints do not restart a transition. System reduced-motion preference is read using the platform API.
- Actual GPUI paint diagnostics and CUA show continuous width/lift transitions, active focus following, and narrow summary-card activation. Hovering a nonactive card expands it without moving the keyboard focus; real `HOVER_焦点仍在原卡` output proves the input target. Leaving ordinary preview restores its pre-hover scroll position; selecting the preview adopts that position. C01/C02 and D01–D07 capture these native paths. CUA's delayed screenshots are settled-state evidence, not animation duration measurements.
- Reference D08–D10 initially appeared not to restore preview position. Reinspection identified the pointer entering the existing edge-intent region. The source starts browsing after 180ms and continues at 900ms intervals; that action intentionally discards the ordinary hover baseline. D15/D16 isolate ordinary reference hover and restore; D17/D18 show the new native edge path keeping its browsing position. The edge indicator and interruption rules are source-derived; complete motion/pixel comparison remains open.
- Core `geometry` retains the source owner validation, safe layout revision/stale rejection, JSON identity no-op, tolerant read/list and invalid-owner pruning. The source-generated sequential contract compares results/errors and actual rows, including close/reopen. Desktop persists root-group scroll/focus/anchor on the source 50ms cadence and flushes before navigation/quit. The generalized source owner formats are ported for future capability wiring; nested groups/DAG/detached-window UI are not accepted yet.
- D07 shows switching away/back retaining the native position. SQLite readback is in `geometry-before-reopen.json`; D11/D12 show process quit/reopen restoring it and accepting `REOPEN_滚动位置保留`. This establishes the simple three-Shell canvas path, not complete hierarchy/window geometry parity.
- D19–D21 were ineffective horizontal automation attempts: diagnostics later showed their macOS horizontal wheel event had zero delta. Do not count them as successful scrolling. A real vertical wheel over the title moves the carousel horizontally (the source-defined behavior). D22 plus `geometry-transient-failure.json` show an isolated SQLite trigger rejecting that position once, then the same desired geometry succeeding 129.1ms later after the trigger is removed, without further user input. The trigger was removed in a finally block. Native horizontal trackpad gestures remain unverified.
- Read-only review found and prompted fixes for immediate wheel/click sampling, stale acknowledgement/retry handling, and restoring only when the column capacity changes. Pending focus intent now suppresses old-focus sampling until its matching authoritative response; errors carry the attempted focus so old failures cannot clear newer intent. Dirty local geometry survives stale database snapshots until its latest matching write succeeds. The latter path is source-reviewed; a sub-120ms roundtrip was not directly injected by CUA. Existing frozen source and production installation/data are unchanged.

Current boundary: root canvas motion/preview/persistence paths are implemented, not a completed canvas parity claim. Tab title truncation, overflow/navigation/close/rename, nested groups, parent pull, full keyboard/mouse/IME, complete layered styling, standalone runtime/process lifetime, and remaining feature groups stay open. Windows has compilation evidence only. No release/install or goal-complete claim.


Final N02w/x validation: a real isolated SQLite writer lock delayed focus confirmation for 6 seconds while CUA clicked the second card then the old tab. The saved anchor still names the second card; returning accepts `RACE_延迟焦点仍正确` there (D23/D24, `geometry-delayed-focus.json`). Ordinary writes now follow source first/immediate-or-50ms continuous cadence and 180ms settled/click debounce; navigation/quit flush immediately. A subsequent same-value-retry probe failed because the render path superseded the event value at a fractional scroll bound (142.93333435058594 versus 142.9333333333334), not because data remained unsaved; that failed record is retained as `geometry-transient-final.json`. Event and render capture now use the same scroll bound. The final build's actual trigger-fault probe succeeds at 127.4ms with exactly the same geometry and no new input (`geometry-transient-clamped.json`, D26). No assertion tolerance was relaxed.

Validation scope: core geometry contract passes; affected desktop tests (real bridge, multi-canvas PTYs and browser motion contract) pass after focus/retry/cadence changes. The final platform-neutral scroll-clamp correction was built and checked in the real GUI; unchanged contracts were not rerun. Windows desktop xwin check passed after focus/retry/cadence changes, with no Windows execution claim. Formatting, identifier policy and diff checks pass. `carousel-build.json` binds the final bundle and current input directory; earlier screenshots retain their explicitly staged interpretation. No complete animation/visual/performance parity claim.

### N02ac — device-scale single-line typography

- Read-only CDP LayerTree snapshots now expose the frozen reference's real `drawTextBlob` positions rather than inferring them from Canvas: English workspace baseline158, Chinese workspace242, task title314 in device pixels. `ui-reference-paint-log.json` preserves layers and complete save/restore/transform command context; `ui-reference-font-geometry.json` binds the same strings and DOM bounds at DPR2. - Root and the read-only advisor resolved the prior contradictory formula: Blink layout coordinates include device-scale zoom. [WebFrameWidget](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/150.0.7871.224/third_party/blink/renderer/core/frame/web_frame_widget_impl.cc#L2184-L2236) supplies that zoom, [FontBuilder](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/150.0.7871.224/third_party/blink/renderer/core/css/resolver/font_builder.cc#L277-L297) scales the font before metric rounding, and [DOM bounds](https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/150.0.7871.224/third_party/blink/renderer/core/layout/adjust_for_absolute_zoom.h#L106-L117) divide it back out. The previous CSS-unit interpretation was incomplete. At2x, SF13 metrics round to25/5, PingFang13 to28/9. Applying upper-half-leading floor and rounded parent origin in these units predicts all three actual paint baselines.

Display output and grid changes follow successful journal records, replay responses are discarded, and theme/cell geometry/synchronized-output deadlines remain coordinated. ### N02ad — tracked-anchor experiment rejects direct adoption

- Actual public tracked-grid-ref probes and frozen SelectionModel execution use identical A/B/C/D input and five mutation scenarios. Bottom append and first-line insert/delete preserve absolute row1 in both systems. For scroll region2–4 followed by bottom newline/E, both screens become A/C/D/E; reference selection remains row1 and copies C, while the native tracked reference moves to row0 and points to A despite remaining valid. Therefore direct use of tracked refs as selection anchors would violate the frozen interaction contract and is rejected.
- With requested history2, reference trim events remove A then B and clear the selected B. - Backup timing boundary: with a 294 MB database the create (backup + integrity + hash) consumed most of the 5 s budget (quit-to-exit ~5.3 s including teardown). Larger databases risk a clean-exit backup timeout, which would still close the DB and exit non-zero with a logged error; recorded as a performance boundary for later.

The recovery-water pointer rule (presses below the card's top 32px while water is shown do not activate/focus/write geometry; header and normal-state presses unchanged) is a faithful replica and was approved under the earlier wording.
### N02bc / L05a — recovery water mounted in the card (implemented; hidden-path evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): per-session `RecoveryWater` entities in `Workspace`, mounted only for visible cards in the current carousel viewport (narrow layout: selected card) and only when `recovery_blocked` is true, the storage-fault set is clear, the recovery state is not Failed, and the node status is not error/exited. Overlay starts at `top: 32px`, clips to the card's 12px bottom radius, hides the body with opacity 0 while mounted, updates with the transport generation and current theme, and is unmounted with image release on Ready/scene change/failure/window hide. Approved pointer rule implemented: while water is mounted, a press at/under 32px inside the card is stopped before focus/activation/geometry; header presses and normal-state presses are unchanged. A visibility watcher polls only while water exists.
- Hidden production-path probe (`transport_generation_probe --recovery-water`, requires `MATOU_RECOVERY_WATER_TRACE=1`; without it the assertions have no rows and abort): layout 560×600 @2 at (270,87) with card top 55 → inset 32; light background `fafcff`, radial center (0.5,0.35)/0.48, edge inset/shadow values; same entity reused across generations 1→2; removed on Ready, hidden window, failed and invalid-path states; mouse `body_at_32_blocked/title_at_16_activates/ready_body_activates_and_focuses` all true; keyboard blocked during recovery and forwarded after Ready; 26 images created and 26 dropped, no in-flight work left. Trace shows ~16.5–18.3 ms frame cadence after the 45 ms first static frame.
- Root real-process check on the isolated package (new build): GUI→runtime lease held; killing the runtime child at t0 produced a new child 156 ms later, the GUI stayed alive and the window stayed on screen, the new runtime took the lease with a new generation, and no clean-exit backup was created (correct for a crash). Real-window water screenshots and on-screen first-frame timing remain unverified because this environment's CUA timed out and screen recording/accessibility are not granted; the mount's visual evidence is the hidden production-path probe only.
- Quit-latency breakdown from the real acceptance plus the hygiene task's measurement: the clean-exit backup itself is fast (210 MB source: create+verify+hash 1.07 s in the core task; the real 294 MB quit still took 4.4–5.3 s end to end). The dominant cost is `ShellOwner::shutdown` stopping each PTY worker sequentially (each path has 1500 ms + 1000 ms stages), while the frozen coordinator shuts sessions down with `Promise.all`. Queued as a runtime node: parallelize per-shell teardown, keep the DB/summary work serial on the owner thread afterwards, then re-measure quit latency with 1/2/4 shells.
### N02bd — backup directory hygiene (implemented; automatic + large-DB evidence) — 2026-11-19

- Root cause confirmed: the online-backup copy inherits the source's WAL header, so the temporary and published databases kept `-wal`/`-shm` and the renamed partial left `.partial-*` orphans. Fix: both backup paths now use the explicit destination connection; after `Backup::step` completes it drops the backup handle, forces a page-1 read, and switches the destination to `PRAGMA journal_mode=DELETE` (verified `delete`, zero busy timeout), failing the backup instead of publishing a dirty artifact; failure cleanup removes sidecars only when the partial WAL is zero bytes, never a non-empty WAL.
- Root independently ran: core full suite (all green; one new `#[ignore]` manual 200 MiB test), backup integration 4+3, the manual large test (`210,010,112 B`, `1.083 s`, two files only, manifest SHA matches, header read/write versions `[1,1]`), and Windows MSVC core check with `RUSTC` set. Existing backups with legacy sidecars are left untouched; only new artifacts are single-file pairs.
- This confirms the earlier real-quit latency (4.4–5.3 s) is not the backup (≈1.5 s at 294 MB) but sequential per-shell teardown; parallel-teardown node is queued with the frozen `Promise.all` comparison.
- Test-hygiene node accepted: the stale `ui_text_ellipsis_probe` now uses the current `SingleLineText { label, .. }` internals (7 access points) and runs again (`work…`, `Dev项目…`, `é…`, `ci👩‍💻…` with the expected 1 px growth after the next grapheme); `transport_generation_probe --recovery-water` enables its own diagnostics so it no longer depends on an external env var; the flaky supervisor test keeps all assertions and only widens the final "new input executed" deadline to 30 s. Root independently ran `cargo check --all-targets`, the probe without `MATOU_RECOVERY_WATER_TRACE`, and the desktop lib suite twice (10/10 each, ~12.7 s). The original flake was not reproduced in three pre-change attempts, so the residual probability is unknown; the wider deadline is a mitigation, not a root-cause proof.
### N02be — parallel session teardown (implemented; real timing evidence) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `SessionWorker::begin_shutdown` now sends the request and returns a `PendingShutdown` (reply receiver + join handle); `ShellOwner::shutdown` sends every active shell's request first, then waits/joins all, while retiring shells are only joined. `shutdown()` keeps its old signature/result semantics (join error first), errors still aggregate to the first failure, and event drain/summary flush stay serial on the owner thread afterwards.
- Real isolated measurements on the 294 MB database (three runs each): before ≈3.11/4.63/7.70 s median for 1/2/4 shells; after ≈3.11/3.13/3.23 s — quit no longer grows linearly with shell count. All nine runs also checked exit codes, shell process exit, backup verification, summaries and lease release.
- Read-only hierarchy/lifecycle study (`hierarchy-plan-01/`): 8 gaps split into data/command/event/UI; the native schema already carries memberships, structural edges, archived fields, run records and fork-intent/worktree tables. Frozen entry is `HierarchyShell → SessionCanvas → TerminalPane/StoppedSessionCard` with `SplitTree` only as fallback; level siblings are `childrenOf(levelParent)` (root = children of none), breadcrumb counts include archived nodes, badge entry focuses the preferred unarchived child (error > needs-input > running/starting > interrupted > idle > exited, ties by graph order), and same-level creation passes the current level parent. Proposed order: levels/focus input → per-card close/remove transactions → authoritative exit/removal events → canvas UI (badges/breadcrumb/stopped/failed cards) → provider fork/worktree. Static evidence only; runtime/DB/UI not exercised in that study.
- Batch A queued from it: level view (root/child siblings), breadcrumb with click/key back, child badges, level-aware geometry keys, and the same-level sibling parent fix. Archived cards keep today's rendering until the lifecycle batch.

Breakdown: teardown now finishes in ~0.15 s (backup request timestamp), the new backup is published as a single `.sqlite`+`.json` pair and verifies (quick_check ok, manifest SHA match, 35 tasks), but `rotate`'s re-verification of the four existing 297 MB backups hits the 5 s clean-exit budget and is interrupted (`clean shutdown backup timed out after 5000ms`, SQLITE_INTERRUPT). The new backup still exists; the child exits non-zero and the GUI quits normally.

Recorded as a shared limitation, no deviation applied; the controlled 1/2/4-shell measurements from the teardown node (3.11/3.13/3.23 s runtime-side) remain the evidence that shell count no longer adds quit time.
### N02bg — per-card close and remove-branch transactions (core/runtime; UI pending) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): new `core/src/hierarchy/cards.rs` with `close_card` and `remove_branch`, plus Host commands `CloseSessionCard`/`RemoveSessionBranch`. Close requires the frozen confirmation intent when the workspace has one unarchived session, a structural child, or running/needs-input work; a sole workspace session returns the frozen notice action (`hide-window` + the exact message) without writing; otherwise it interrupts the owner's own active run inside the transaction, archives the session, detaches scene windows, removes the mount and activates the preferred sibling (current focus, else earliest mount), or for the last session either keeps the canvas (`preserve_scene_on_last_session`) or archives scene→task→(preferred successor/default task). Results carry `outcome`, `disposedSessionIds`, `rebuildCanvas`. Remove-branch keeps at least one membership, archives and unmounts the target(s), revokes touching relations, reparents direct children in `sibling_created_seq` order for `node-only`, deletes memberships, updates focus via parent→child→most-recent remaining, and never deletes session rows, project files or worktrees. Host retires only the target workers, waits for their actual exit, then publishes `ScenesDisposed` before the authoritative `Navigation`/`Graph` in command order while other shell traffic keeps flowing.
- Verification: core/runtime full suites 125 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (pre-existing + the manual large-backup test); root reran them plus Mac and Windows MSVC `--all-targets`, fmt, identifiers and whitespace. The new real-child test drives two shells, checks both remove scopes and the close path, asserts the target PID is already gone when disposal is published while the survivor still answers input, verifies stale-intent rejection leaves both runs untouched, and checks the last-card notice writes nothing.
- Still open: desktop menu/shortcut/confirmation dialog and stopped-card UI (next gate), provider fork/worktree, Windows live PTY, and real visible-window acceptance.
- **Accessibility gap (global):** GPUI in this pinned vendor build exposes no ARIA/native accessibility label API, so controls that have `aria-label` in the frozen product (card actions, dialogs, scope choices, recovery controls) currently deliver only visible tooltips and keyboard operation. This is a real parity gap for screen-reader users, not waived: it needs a separate vendor/accessibility bridge node if the product requires VoiceOver parity. Do not fake labels with element IDs or hidden text; future UI nodes must list which controls still lack accessible names.

### N02bj — real Claude provider session design (static design accepted; no product code yet) — 2026-11-19

(b) The user has **no usable API key right now**, so anything that needs a real model turn (identity confirmation, resume by ID, context read-back) is blocked on a credential — either a dedicated test key or one operator login inside the isolated root. Launch-side facts that need no credential (real CLI argv/env, isolated root files, `--version` capability probe, missing-command failure card) can be accepted first.

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `RecoveryState` gains `StartFailed(reason)` and `Restarting`; a launch failure is a start failure for a session with no prior run and a recovery failure (`Failed`, existing restore card) for a session that already has run records, and the reason is now the real error (`start Shell <path>: No such file or directory`, workspace-path reasons) instead of "did not reach a running state"; the same text goes to `Diagnostic` but the desktop keeps it on the card instead of the global fault banner. `Command::RestartSession { session_id }` accepts only a non-archived Shell in the current scene with the start-failed or exited status, no live/retiring worker and no pending launch; it re-runs the existing directory choice + `launch_shell` path, is idempotent while a retry is in flight, and re-fails into the same card with the newer reason. `RetryRecovery` keeps its old meaning (recovery rebuild only). Desktop: frozen `会话启动失败` card (`#9b2f2a` on `#fff3f2`, `#edc2bf` borders, reason `#a95853`, `rgba(228,93,85,.45)` button border, `rgba(228,93,85,.08)` background, `#f2b7b3` text, 11px/normal, 8×10 padding, 520px container switch to a two-line reason), buttons 「重试启动」/「移除失败会话」 with frozen blocked reasons (read-only DB, environment, transport, storage) and no colour/opacity dimming when blocked; 移除失败会话 sends `CloseSessionCard` with `delete-session:<id>`.

- Three review corrections were sent back and fixed: the invented 「正在启动…」 label and the card staying visible during retry (frozen switches to `starting-session` and the card disappears), the button border having to be the frozen half-transparent colour with no invented disabled dimming, and the card/reason line height (frozen uses `normal`, ~13.2px).
- Correction to the executor's last open item: the frozen loading presentation is the same recovery-water overlay with a `loading` phase (`terminal-loading-state.ts` → `SessionRecoveryWater phase="loading"`), and its text exists only as `aria-label` (`正在加载终端：<title>`) plus a visually hidden detail — native already mounts the water for a not-yet-ready card (`recovery_blocked` is true until `Ready`, so a first launch is covered), and the visible surface therefore matches. What is genuinely missing is the accessible label/detail, which the pinned GPUI build cannot expose at all (global accessibility gap, not a new node). Real Claude Code 2.1.328 launches from an empty config root and its own PTY screen shows `Welcome to Claude Code v2.1.328`; PID, loaded executable, empty argv and cwd are verified from `lsof`/`ps`, exactly one run row is committed as `running`, `Ctrl+C` twice ends the process (real exit code 1, no signal), the row becomes `exited`, no Shell history block is written, and the worker joins on runtime shutdown. Five failure cases (`missing-command`, `non-executable` → `Permission denied (os error 13)`, `missing-cwd` → `No such file or directory (os error 2)`, `missing-binding`, corrupt config → `invalid JSON at line 1, column 11`) each emit a `StartFailed` whose text equals the diagnostic and leave no run or child. ### N02bm — Claude Code identity: loopback hooks and provider binding (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): new `runtime/src/provider_hook.rs` starts one loopback receiver per owner (`127.0.0.1:0`), registers each run under a 32-byte random base64url token, accepts only POST JSON objects up to 1 MiB with a 1.5 s request deadline and 8 connections, and never logs tokens or bodies. Per run it writes `<data_root>/provider-hooks/<token>.settings.json` (0700 dir, 0600 file, temp+rename) with the frozen nine hook events and 5/3/10 s timeouts plus a `command` statusline (`/bin/sh` + `/usr/bin/curl --max-time 2`), and `--settings <path>` is the first argv element. Identity comes from the hook payload's `session_id`/`sessionId`/`conversationId`/`conversation_id` or the transcript-path UUID; `SessionEnd` and statusline-only reports cannot confirm a fresh session; events from a retired run, a different generation or a mismatched expected ID are rejected. Retirement withdraws claim authority immediately, keeps the endpoint for the frozen 2 s grace, then deletes the files; runtime shutdown clears the directory. Confirmation writes the binding through the existing transaction/dedup path (`core::sessions::record_claude_identity`): idempotent by `(session, provider, provider_session_id)`, `resume_state='available'`, `validated_at`, cleared `invalidated_at`/`restore_error`, and metadata with `providerConfigId`/`model`/`permissionMode`/`cwd`/`lastHookEvent`/`observedUserPrompt`/`observedNormalStop`. Before confirmation a provider run's output is displayed and journalled but does not move the card summary, cwd, work status or notifications (a provider run's exit also stops owning `work_status` until an identity was recorded); confirmation/rejection/timeout reach the desktop as graph events.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; DeepSeek provider from the user's own Matou config, key used only inside the isolated root): with hooks on, the real CLI 2.1.328 answers a real prompt, the `UserPromptSubmit` event carries the CLI's own session ID, the database binding carries the identical ID in `available`, `Stop` is observed, and the hook directory is empty afterwards; with hooks disabled the CLI still answers but the runtime reports an identity timeout, writes zero bindings and leaves derived state untouched. Neither run leaks the credential (artifact scan + `credential_absent_from_artifacts`).
- Root also reran: isolated root removed, core+runtime 137 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers.
- Decisions: derived output before confirmation is discarded rather than buffered; a shared receiver is created on the first Claude run with a 10 s identity timeout while the run can still confirm later; `127.0.0.1` is added to the no-proxy list when a proxy is configured; the isolation case allows outbound network only for the authorized endpoint, the local proxy and DNS (documented in the evidence) while file access stays denied.
- Open: `--resume`/restore scheduling and provider restore UI, subscription/router branch, provider work status and notifications from hook events, `--plugin-dir`, Windows live CLI and file-access control, visible-window pixels.
### N02bn — Claude Code resume, expected identity and restore parking (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): a session whose latest binding is `available`, validated and not invalidated launches with `--resume <provider_session_id>` in the frozen argv position (after settings/model/effort, before the permission flags) and registers that ID as the expected identity. Restore confirmation requires a current-run authoritative lifecycle hook whose ID is byte-equal to the expected one; `SessionEnd`, statusline-only reports and unknown events never confirm, mismatches revoke the run's derivative rights, retire the hook and stop the PTY while leaving the original binding intact. Four failure classes park the binding (`resume_state='failed'`, `restore_state='failed'`, `restore_error`, invalidated time): spawn failure → `provider process could not start: <real error>`, exit before confirmation while monitoring → `provider resume exited with code <code>`, CLI "session not found" / "running in the background" text scoped to the expected ID → the frozen reasons, and identity mismatch → the frozen mismatch reason. Parked bindings are never retried automatically (startup skips them); the 10 s deadline releases the launch queue only — the identity gate stays closed. `RetryProviderRestore` clears the failure, bumps `spawnRevision` and reuses the same ID; `StartFreshProviderSession` expires the old binding and launches without `--resume`. The resume monitor mirrors the frozen marker sets for the full-resume prompt (current → Enter, legacy → Down+Enter), the workspace-trust prompt (Down, 500 ms, Enter, 150 ms) and failure matching after ANSI normalisation.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI 2.1.328 + DeepSeek, loopback-only network through a proxy that permits only the authorised endpoint): round 1 answers `OK` with an `available` binding; after a clean runtime shutdown and restart the new process launches with the same ID in argv, a different generation and the identity confirmed only by the read-back prompt; the read-back answer equals the random token from round 1, the transcript and binding are the same row. Removing the conversation reproduces `provider session not found`, parks the binding, and a restart does not retry; `RetryProviderRestore` adds exactly one run with the same ID and `spawnRevision 1`; `StartFreshProviderSession` produces a new ID with no `--resume` and expires the old binding; a wrong-ID hook stops the live PTY and never overwrites the stored identity; the timeout case releases the queue while preserving summary/work status and the binding count; a missing executable parks `provider process could not start: 未检测到 …`.
- Root also reran: core+runtime 137 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace, identifiers, and a credential scan of all artifacts (0 hits).
- Open: provider restore UI (banner/cards/buttons), subscription and router branch, provider work status/notifications from hook events, real "running in the background" reproduction (unit-tested only), `--plugin-dir`, Windows live, visible pixels. Note: the real first authoritative confirmation is the `UserPromptSubmit` of the read-back prompt; no `SessionStart` was observed before it, matching the frozen comment.
### N02bo — provider restore banner and explicit restore actions (desktop; hidden-path evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

### N02bp — provider work status from terminal input and hooks (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): any session's submitted terminal input (`\r`/`\n`, including an empty Enter) sets `running`; hooks classify `Stop` → completed, `PermissionRequest` → permission and `Notification` by the frozen eight-field first-non-empty text with case-insensitive permission/completed/waiting keyword matching (else attention); completed → `idle`, everything else → `needs-input`, `error` stays sticky against hooks, and only the current, identity-confirmed, non-retired run of a non-archived session can change status (statusline/unknown events ignored). Submissions and hooks carry monotonic timestamps so a queued `Stop` cannot overwrite a newer Enter. Writes go through the existing `session.work-status` command/dedup and reach the desktop through the normal graph publication; exit keeps `finish_run`.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI 2.1.328 + DeepSeek): a real answer produces `running → idle` in the graph, an injected `PermissionRequest` on the live run's hook URL produces `needs-input`, a pre-set `error` survives an injected `Stop` (status and version unchanged), and the process exit reads back `exited`. A genuine `idle_prompt` notification was sampled: `message = "Claude is waiting for your input"`, matching the frozen waiting keywords.
- Root also reran core+runtime 140 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers.
- Known small deviation: a hook that *confirms* a brand-new identity (for example the first `UserPromptSubmit`) does not also apply its notification status; frozen applies it after accepting side effects. In practice the confirming event is a prompt submission, which yields no notification status, so the visible result matches; revisit if a confirming `Stop` ever occurs.
- Open: desktop dot/badge visuals built on this status, notification centre (SID-12/13), provider restore UI (accepted separately in N02bo), fork/loader, Windows live.
### N02bq — Claude session catalog and loading an older conversation (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): new `runtime/src/provider_catalog.rs` reads Claude Code's own project transcripts under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`~/.claude` + `/projects` without writing anything: it tries the encoded `resolve(cwd)` name and the encoded canonical cwd, canonicalises every candidate and requires containment in the projects root, re-checks inode/device after opening (symlink swaps are skipped and counted), accepts only `<provider-session-id>.jsonl`, parses `cwd`/title/autoTitle/model/permissionMode/timestamps (SQLite's ISO-8601 parser) and keeps only transcripts whose recorded cwd canonicalises to the target, skips malformed or >8 MiB lines with counters, and returns up to 200 entries sorted by `updatedAt` with a stable tie-break plus `loadedBy` (the newest available, non-archived binding that currently holds that conversation). New commands: `ListProviderSessions { cwd }` (results ride the existing graph event) and `LoadProviderSession { session_id, provider_session_id }`, which validates card/scene/cwd, rejects a card with a running process, expires the previous binding for that card in the same transaction, writes a provisional `loadedFromCatalog` binding with catalog model/permission and `spawnRevision + 1`, then starts the existing `--resume` path; the load only completes when the current run's hook confirms that ID, otherwise it parks like any restore. Duplicate loads start one run; closing the card mid-load rejects and reclaims the process.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI 2.1.328 + DeepSeek, isolated workspace path containing Chinese characters and a space): two real conversations were seeded, the catalog listed exactly those two for that cwd (another cwd returns empty) and left the transcripts untouched; loading the chosen ID launched `--resume <ID>`, the hook confirmed the same ID, and the read-back answer equals the token from that older conversation; a lookup for a missing ID is rejected with no binding or run, and an ID that disappears between lookup and resume produces the real `provider session not found` parking; `loadedBy` reads back; duplicate loads add no run; the previous binding is expired.
- Root also reran core+runtime 146 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace, identifiers and a credential scan (0 hits).

### N02bt — loading a conversation replaces a running card (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `LoadProviderSession` first confirms the requested history exists in the card's catalog, then stops the card's running process, waits for the real exit (exit code/signal recorded, worker reclaimed), and only then commits the new binding and starts `--resume` with identity confirmation. A failure to stop or to persist the exit returns the original error, keeps the binding unchanged and adds no run. Concurrent loads of the same conversation run once; loading a conversation the card already has confirmed returns an explicit `confirmed` identity event with `reason: "already-loaded"` (no new run). Closing the card during the stop cancels the load and reclaims the process. Input and auto-restart for that card are blocked during the stop.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI + DeepSeek): with the CLI mid-answer, a load made the old PID disappear, its run was recorded `exited` with `exit_code 129`, a new run started with `--resume`, its identity was confirmed and the older conversation's token read back; an injected SQLite exit-write failure left the process reaped, bindings unchanged and zero new runs. Root also reran core+runtime 151 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers; credential scan clean.
- Desktop follow-up (fixed in the same node): the loader previously required a newer `spawnRevision`, so the same-revision `already-loaded` success was ignored and the dialog could stay open. It now closes on a same-card, same-conversation `confirmed` with `reason: already-loaded` while other confirmations still require a newer revision, with a probe assertion for the same-revision event (no resend). Root reran desktop 27, the `--session-loader` probe (33.8 ms for the 200-row draw), the workspace check, fmt (one probe-format fix applied by the root), whitespace and identifiers.
### N02bu — fork a child session in the current worktree (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `ForkSession { window_id, scene_id, source_session_id, placement: child, name, worktree_mode: current, submission_key }` validates scene membership, archive state, non-detached mount, a valid `available` binding with a completed turn (`canFork`, derived from recorded prompt/Stop for older bindings while honouring an explicit false), the frozen name rules and a non-empty submission key, with the frozen error codes/messages (`DETACHED_SOURCE`, `ROOT_HAS_NO_FORK_PARENT`, `FORK_SOURCE_NOT_READY`). In one transaction it creates the child session inheriting cwd/provider/task/execution context with the requested title, mounts it to the right of the source, writes the `forked-from` relation and records the fork intent (reusing the existing intent table, marked as native), replaying the same result for a repeated submission key. The launch uses `--resume <source> --fork-session` under a fork-specific authority: only an authoritative lifecycle identity **different** from the source confirms the fork and writes the child binding; the source id, a spawn failure, an early exit, or the frozen **60 s** fork identity timeout keep the card in a failure state with the real reason and write no binding. Placement peer/sibling and worktree `new` return explicit not-implemented errors rather than silently degrading. A waiting fork does not occupy the ordinary restore concurrency.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI 2.1.328 + DeepSeek): a source conversation with a completed turn; the fork launched with `--resume <source> --fork-session`, sat idle for 12.1 s without a binding (waiting for the first message, as the frozen 60 s rule allows), then the first `UserPromptSubmit` reported a **new** id and the child read back the source's full token; a repeated submission reused the same card and run; the source's session, binding, run and summary text were unchanged (only its terminal redraw advanced the summary timestamp); an incomplete source turn was rejected with `FORK_SOURCE_NOT_READY`; is an isolated root, credential scan clean.

### N02bv — sibling and peer fork placements (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `ForkSession` now accepts all three frozen placements and follows their distinct semantics — `child` forks from the selected card and parents the new card to it; `sibling` forks from the *selected card's parent* (argv resumes the parent's conversation) and parents the new card to that parent; `peer` forks from the selected card but attaches the new card to the selected card's parent with a `derived-from` relation, becoming a root when the selected card has none. All three reuse the creation transaction, current-run identity confirmation, the 60 s fork identity window, failure parking and submission-key replay; a sibling request from a root returns `ROOT_HAS_NO_FORK_PARENT` (「根层会话可创建子分支」), a dangling parent relation returns `FORK_SOURCE_NOT_READY` for sibling and becomes a root for peer, and a running parent is allowed as long as its binding is usable. Name conflicts are checked per target level for sibling/peer.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI + DeepSeek): a parent conversation (token `BAD0…`) and a child source (token `FEE7…`) were created first; the sibling fork launched with `--resume <parent id> --fork-session`, landed under the parent and read back the **parent's** token `BAD0A88D603D33EF`; the peer fork launched with `--resume <source id> --fork-session`, landed beside the source under the same parent with `derived-from` and read back the **source's** token `FEE7CB28B6541E9E`; both confirmed independent ids, replays created no extra runs, the original cards' bindings/status/runs were unchanged, and the root sibling request returned the frozen error.
- Root also reran core+runtime 155 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers.
- Open: Git new-worktree mode, the desktop fork entry/dialog/failure card, an explicit fork-retry command (not implemented yet), fork progress presentation, Windows live.

### N02by — permission mode projection and switching (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): graph nodes now carry `permissionMode` (default `"default"`) and `model` from the latest valid provider binding (no binding → default/absent), and `SetProviderPermissionMode { session_id, mode }` rejects invalid modes, non-Claude sessions, archived cards, cards without an active binding and cards that are stopping/recovering. A switch retires the old hook, stops the current run and waits for its real exit record, writes the new permission into the binding metadata, relaunches with `--resume <same conversation id>` plus the mode's flag, requires the 150 ms replacement check and identity confirmation of the same conversation, and on any failure restores the previous permission and returns the real error (identity failures keep the existing parking flow). Same-value switches succeed without a restart; concurrent identical requests share one result. For the first switch into Bypass Permissions the CLI's own "No, exit / Yes, I accept" prompt is shown in the terminal: only arrow keys/Enter/Esc are released while that prompt is recognised (with the identity timeout paused), everything else stays intercepted, and choosing No fails the switch with the CLI's real reason. Results ride the existing graph channel as `providerPermissionModeResults`. One deliberate deviation: the settings for a permission respawn replace the (unused) HTTP `SessionStart` entry with a local command hook forwarding the same payload to the same run URL, because this CLI version skips HTTP startup hooks and the switch holds input until identity is known; every other run keeps the frozen HTTP-only settings.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI + DeepSeek): `plan` (argv `--permission-mode plan`) resumed the same conversation, confirmed the same id and read back the previous token; `bypassPermissions` (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`) waited ~11 s at the CLI's own risk prompt, accepted it, confirmed the same conversation; `default` (no permission flag) confirmed the same conversation; the previous run had exited before each new one, and the binding id stayed the same.
- Root also reran core+runtime 161 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers. Frozen graph fixtures predating the new field are compared with a documented default-field shim, and a dedicated test asserts the new projection.

### N02ca — window visibility diagnostic (debug-only; finding recorded) — 2026-11-19

- Added a debug-build, `MATOU_WINDOW_DIAG`-gated window diagnostic (`main.rs`, `macos.rs`, a first-paint hook in `workspace.rs`; `1` = snapshots, `2` = also an opaque-background visibility probe). Default builds and runs are unaffected (no output when unset or `0`).
- Finding on this machine: the acceptance app's window is fully healthy in AppKit — `activationPolicy=0`, `NSWindow.isVisible=true`, `occlusionState=0x2000`, frame 1200×780 inside the single 1512×982 screen, first paint at 1200×780, `GPUIView` mounted in `contentView.subviews` with `wantsLayer=true` and a `CAMetalLayer` (drawable 2400×1560, `nextDrawable` available in ~1 ms), display link at 119 callbacks/s with ~100 GPUI frame requests/s — yet the window never appears on the display: full-screen captures show only the wallpaper, `kCGWindowSharingState=0`, `kCGWindowMemoryUsage` stays ~2 KB, `screencapture -l` refuses, and forcing an **opaque magenta** window background (`DIAG=2`) changes nothing on screen (two captures pixel-identical). `NSApp.isActive=false` and `keyWindow/mainWindow` are nil throughout.
- Conclusion so far: this is not a "not ordered front", "zero-size content" or "transparent content" problem; the window is being created and rendered but the WindowServer does not composite it on the captured display. Next check requires the user: launching the same bundle from Finder and reporting whether it becomes visible, which separates "launch context of the agent's shell" from "machine-level compositing".
### N02cb — permission switch on a card without a resumable binding (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): `SetProviderPermissionMode` now also accepts a Claude card with no usable binding. It stops a running process if present, expires any invalidated/failed binding, launches a **new** conversation (no `--resume`) with the requested permission, and only after the replacement check and identity confirmation writes the new id and mode into an `available` binding. A first identity that merely repeats a historical card id is rejected so an old binding cannot silently come back; a start or identity failure returns the real error and leaves the old binding expired. Launches reuse the previous binding's provider/model while the permission comes from the request.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI + DeepSeek): from zero bindings, switching to `plan` launched with `--permission-mode plan` and **no** `--resume`, confirmed a new conversation and bound it; switching back to `default` then launched with `--resume <that new id>` (exact argv verified) and DeepSeek read the earlier token back from the same conversation; the regression path with an existing binding (plan, first bypass with the CLI's own consent, default) still resumes the original conversation.

### N02cc — provider notifications as domain events (real-CLI evidence accepted) — 2026-11-19

- Implementation (root-reviewed): qualifying hooks (`Stop`, `PermissionRequest`, `Notification`) now persist one `agent.notification` domain event through the existing transaction/dedup path and the newest payloads ride `Graph.agentNotifications` (no protocol bump, no schema change). `Stop` → completed with subtitle `Completed in <cwd tail>` and body from `last_assistant_message`/`lastAssistantMessage`/`result` else the frozen completion text (200 UTF-16 units, cooldown `Stop`); `PermissionRequest` → `<tool>: <detail>` or `<tool> requires approval` (180 units, cooldown `Notification`); `Notification` classified permission/completed/waiting/attention by the frozen keyword order. Title is `Claude Code`, `sound: true`, replacement key omitted as in the frozen contract, and payloads carry workspace/task/session ownership plus millisecond `occurredAt`. Notifications are dropped for unconfirmed identities, expected-id mismatches, non-current runs, old generations, retired or archived sessions, `SessionEnd`, statusline and unknown events.
- Real acceptance (root reran the harness independently; real CLI 2.1.328 + DeepSeek): a real answer produced one completed notification whose body equals the model's reply, visible identically in SQLite and on the client; an injected `PermissionRequest` produced one permission notification with `Bash: printf notification-test`; a `Stop` injected for a retired run produced zero notifications in both places.
- Root also reran core+runtime 166 passed / 2 ignored, desktop 27, workspace all-target check, Windows MSVC all-target check, fmt, whitespace and identifiers.
- Recorded deviation: when the frozen UTF-16 truncation would split a surrogate pair, native emits U+FFFD for that single character instead of a lone surrogate (same length and ellipsis; copying the reference's broken output has no user value).

### Goal alignment — migrate against current `main` (2026-11-19, user request)

Runtime payloads now carry `sceneId` (internal delivery field only — no new command, protocol or schema change), resolved from the navigable mount preferring the window's most recent activation then mount creation time; older payloads fall back to the received graph and window navigation records.

### Window visibility: environment-level, not app-specific (2026-11-19)

- Reference baseline rebuilt from main@924de28d: `pnpm package:dir` produced the bundle (signing with the production identity failed only on a Windows `OpenConsole.exe` entitlement read; a separate unsigned build via `electron-builder --dir --config.mac.identity=null` was used instead so no production-signed artifact is created). The unsigned bundle is stored as `artifacts/native-parity/baseline/MatouReference-924de28d.app` (0.2.24, `com.matou.desktop` before isolation), and `launch-reference.py`/`isolate-reference-home.cjs` now accept `--baseline`/explicit baseline and reference paths so old evidence keeps its default location.
- Launching that Electron reference app from the agent session reproduces exactly the native-app symptom: the process runs, the menu bar names the app as frontmost, but full-screen captures show only the wallpaper — with `open -a` as well as direct execution. Combined with the native window's healthy AppKit state (visible, unoccluded, first paint done, Metal drawable available) and the identical result for a completely different GUI stack, the conclusion is that windows created by processes launched from this agent session are not composited on the user's display. This is an environment/session property, not a defect of the native app; the earlier "window never paints" diagnosis is withdrawn.
- Consequence: real-window pixel/IME/scrollbar acceptance cannot be driven by processes this session launches. It needs the user to launch the app themselves (or grant the agent's terminal both Screen Recording and Accessibility and confirm a launched window is visible), after which the agent can capture/drive.
### Real-window IME acceptance (native, macOS, 2026-11-19) — first real evidence; earlier paste "defect" withdrawn

- After the user reset the macOS privacy permissions, `screencapture` and `System Events` work from this session. The native acceptance app (`artifacts/native-parity/native/MatouNative.app`, isolated data root) now shows its real window and accepts real keyboard input. Evidence: `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/ime-real-01/`.
- Verified on the real window with the system IME (Sogou Pinyin): pinyin letters produce an underlined preedit in the terminal with the candidate window (`ni'hao` → candidates 你好 …); `Esc` cancels the preedit and leaves the prompt unchanged; `Space` commits the candidate (你好 inserted); ASCII paste via Cmd+V inserts the clipboard text (`ABC123`).
- **Withdrawn 2026-11-19: the claim below was a test-harness artifact, not a product defect.** In this session's shell `LANG` is empty/`LC_CTYPE=C`, and `printf 中文 | pbcopy` there writes **zero bytes** to the pasteboard (reproduced: `pbpaste | wc -c` = 0), so nothing could paste anywhere. With the clipboard actually populated (`osascript -e 'set the clipboard to …'` or `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8 pbcopy`) the unmodified package pastes Chinese, emoji and multiline text correctly. A second artifact: an acceptance build launched with a C locale makes zsh's own line editor render CJK as `?<00ad>` and the ZWJ as `<200d>`; a direct pty run of zsh shows the same bytes, so that display is the shell, not the terminal.
  - Original (invalid) wording kept for the record: "pasting non-ASCII text does nothing … the native terminal's paste path loses non-ASCII clipboard text." Evidence `native-paste-cn.png` / `native-paste-cn2.png` shows an empty clipboard, not a dropped paste.
- Real, narrower defect found by the follow-up study and fixed: when the source app offers only `public.utf16-plain-text`, the system's synthesized UTF-8 representation starts with `EF BB BF` (U+FEFF); GPUI read that and the terminal wrote the invisible BOM into the PTY (a pasted command then fails). AppKit's `stringForType(NSPasteboardTypeString)` returns the BOM-free text, which is also what the frozen Electron `clipboard.readText()` uses. Evidence and byte captures: `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/paste-fix-01/`; root re-verified the synthesized-UTF-8 BOM with an independent Swift probe and captured real PTY bytes from a `cat > file` paste (18 bytes, byte-identical to the clipboard).
- Evidence captured: `native-preedit2.png`, `native-escape.png`, `native-commit2.png`, `native-paste3.png` (ASCII OK), `native-paste-cn.png` / `native-paste-cn2.png` (empty clipboard, not a defect).
- Paste path as shipped (commit `e1790674`, real-window verified on an isolated build launched with `LANG=en_US.UTF-8`): the terminal and the rename input share one reader — AppKit's plain-text representation first, GPUI only when AppKit has no text. `encode_paste` now matches the frozen xterm helpers line for line (CRLF/LF → CR; bracketed mode wraps with `ESC[200~`/`ESC[201~`; ESC/C1 CSI/marker bytes in the clipboard are preserved instead of being stripped — previously a pasted ANSI log lost its escapes and a lone ESC sent nothing). Real PTY captures: Chinese, UTF-16+ZWJ (no `EF BB BF`), ANSI text in bracketed mode, lone ESC in both modes, plus the same UTF-16 text pasted into the rename field and read back byte-identical. Desktop tests, fmt, identifiers and whitespace checks pass.
- Accepted limitations (documented, not fixed): a pasteboard whose `public.utf8-plain-text` holds invalid UTF-8 falls back to GPUI's lossy decode and inserts U+FFFD where the frozen product inserts nothing; the new AppKit unit test needs a working pasteboard service, so it is a Mac-desktop test only.
- Still open: the same sequence on the frozen reference app for a paired comparison; held-scrollbar preedit gesture; continuous scrollbar drag/fade/interruption; visible-window pixel comparisons.
