### Model-role routing (R01–R03) — study done, gate A in progress (2026-11-20)

- Read-only study `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/routing-study-01/` maps R01–R04/R09 against the native provider stack with file:line evidence. Adjudicated scope: ship the **startup closure** first — per-tier provider/model/effort assignments for new sessions — and defer the same-card request gateway, subscription catalogue, search routing, explicit global apply, context budgets and multi-channel egress. Persistence reuses `preferences['agent.modelPreferences']` (frozen shape) with no SQL DDL and no writes to `provider-config.json`.
- Gate A (working tree, in rework) adds the preference store, save validation and a new-session launch projection. Reviewer findings being fixed: empty/alias starting models bypassed assignments; the built-in official target could never be assigned without stored metadata; launch skipped validation; retry after a failed first launch silently fell back to the default provider; partial/invalid stored JSON was used instead of falling back to defaults; the write connection bypassed `configure_connection`.
- **Blocker found by root while adjudicating:** the assignment gate keys on `kind='claude-code' AND status='created'`, but in the native app every creation path writes `kind='shell'`; a session only becomes `claude-code` together with `status='starting'` at the identity hook, and forks/loads carry bindings. So today no user action can start a fresh Claude session through that guard — earlier Claude-launch acceptance seeded the row (`verify-claude-launch.py:403`). The frozen RPC accepts `session.create` with a `kind`, so the missing native surface is "create a card as Claude Code"; the exact frozen UI entry is not yet confirmed and is the next study.

### Model-role assignments (R01–R04, R09) — gate A landed (2026-11-20)

- Entry study (`artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/claude-card-entry-01/`): the frozen desktop has no "new Claude card" command. Users create an ordinary Shell card and type `claude` or `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions`; the runtime buffers the line, declines to give it to the shell and promotes the same card/session id to `claude-code` in place (`apps/runtime/src/runtime-server.ts:4419-4475`, `4861-4900`). Native has no interception yet, so no user action can currently reach a fresh `claude-code` launch; the Shell-promotion gate is the next step and will also give the assignment gate its real entry.
- Gate A (landed): preferences stay in the existing `agent.modelPreferences` row (whole-row validation; any invalid sibling falls back to the defaults wholesale; no DDL, no `provider-config.json` writes) and are saved with the same target validation used at launch. A launch that is a new conversation (no `--resume`, no fork, no permission restart) resolves the saved assignment of its starting tier; the starting tier comes from the currently active provider's model (`''`/`default`/`CLI 默认` → Opus, the CLI 2.1.328 default verified by the real `SessionStart` hook), CLI aliases mirror frozen `claudeRequestRole`, several tiers mapped to one model take the first in opus→sonnet→haiku order, and a matched launch passes explicit `--model`/`--effort` on the assigned provider with that tier's `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_<ROLE>_MODEL` and Fable mirroring Opus. Inherited Claude model/subagent variables are cleared whenever assignments exist. The assigned role and effort are written into the binding metadata by identity confirmation, so resume and permission restarts keep the same projection; a permission change before identity reuses the prepared target instead of the stale binding.
- Verification (root reran independently): `cargo test -p matou-native-core -p matou-native-runtime` 197 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored; `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `pnpm check:identifiers`, `git diff --check` clean. A root-written black-box script (`/tmp/matou-verify-routing/verify.py`: real runtime binary + stub CLI, own temp data roots, no user data) passed six scenarios — stale binding to a deleted provider still uses the assignment, no assignments source the active provider, three tiers on one model take the Opus assignment, a lone Sonnet assignment clears inherited Claude env, a permission change before identity keeps the prepared target/effort, and resume keeps the bound provider. Earlier real-CLI evidence (`runs/matou-role-routing-a3-01/logs/real-cli.log`) showed the omitted-`--model` default `claude-opus-5-5` and explicit `--model`/`--effort` on assigned launches.
- Not yet verified: desktop settings UI for assignments (later gate), the real `/model` menu inside a real window, and an actual provider request. Known limits (deliberate): native has no model catalog, so fallback-tier and official member validation are skipped and subscription assignment targets are rejected; cross-tier switching and per-tier subagent routing need the request gateway; when the starting tier is unassigned, restart effort is recomputed and the assignment env-clearing flag lives only in process memory.

### Shell → Claude in-place promotion (N02ch) — landed 2026-11-20

- Entry (frozen `apps/runtime/src/runtime-server.ts:4419-4475,4861-4900`): typing `claude` or `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions` in an ordinary Shell card and pressing Enter turns that card into a Claude card in place — same session id, same canvas mount, same focus, title `Shell`/id → `Claude`, user titles kept; the command never reaches the shell.
- Buffer semantics are the frozen ones, verified character by character: every `\r` and every `\n` submits separately (CRLF therefore does not promote), only the last submission of one write is matched, a match withholds the entire write from the shell (prefix commands never run) and sends only Ctrl+U, a miss forwards the write verbatim, backspace removes one UTF-16 code unit (an emoji needs two), Ctrl+C/Ctrl+U clear, input is decoded as a UTF-8 stream across writes.
- Promotion is serialized per session: stop the old shell, one transaction that only updates the existing row and title, invalidates any old binding and its fork flags, then a fresh launch with the run's own settings/hooks, **no `--resume` and no borrowed binding**, permission from the command (bypass → `--dangerously-skip-permissions`), and the saved model-role assignment applied as a new conversation. A failed launch returns the same card to a usable Shell without a binding or dialog. Keystrokes typed during the switch are replayed to the new PTY; submissions decide per session so other terminals and shutdown stay responsive; a detached phase answers copy with an empty reply instead of stalling the UI; at shutdown only promotions whose process check succeeded stay Claude (others restore the shell, status untouched).
- Verification (root ran these): `cargo test -p matou-native-core -p matou-native-runtime` 225 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored; `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `pnpm check:identifiers`, `git diff --check` clean. A root black-box script (real runtime + real zsh + stub CLI, own temp roots) passed seven cases: `claude⏎` promotes, CRLF does not, `claude⏎ls⏎` does not, a write ending in `claude⏎` promotes without running anything else, one/two backspaces around an emoji, and the bypass command. The repository's real-CLI script passed all three modes against Claude Code 2.1.328 (`apps/native/target/shell-promotion-real.json`): same card/mount/focus, spawned argv contains only the run's private `--settings` (plus `--dangerously-skip-permissions` in bypass) with no `--resume`, and the loopback hook recorded a real conversation identity for the new run.
- Not yet verified: the three desktop scenarios by hand, a real logged-in account's bypass confirmation screen, Intel Mac. Accepted gaps: an ordinary (non-promoted) Claude card can still drop a hook that arrives in the last milliseconds of shutdown (pre-existing shutdown drain scope); the verified-but-not-Ready keep path is exercised through a debug-build test hook only; the both-writes-failed clear path has no end-to-end test (unit coverage only).

### Assignment settings UI — study done, UI deferred behind provider management (2026-11-20)

- Read-only study `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/assignment-ui-01/` (`ui.md`, `flow.md`, `result.json`, 216 citations) records the frozen 「设置 → Claude Code → 模型分配」 screen against `924de28d`: the settings panel is a full-area page inside the right workspace (left nav 「AI 服务 / Claude Code / 通用 / 语言与时区」, header, close button, Escape-to-close, entrance animation); the Claude page contains 服务供应商 cards, the assignment block, 对话压缩模型 and 派活; the assignment block is four rows (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku/网页搜索) with provider+account, model (catalog or manual), reasoning effort, per-row validation copy, one draft shared by 恢复默认/保存分配, and browser-native `select` popups for which only source-level layout is known.
- Decision (root): do not ship the settings UI before provider management. Native still only reads `provider-config.json`; a page with this layout but without adding/switching/editing providers, and with a 网页搜索 row the runtime cannot route yet, would be a visibly incomplete copy of the frozen screen. The next settings gate therefore starts with provider management and the authorization to write `provider-config.json` (a product trade-off to raise with the user at that point), then the page shell, then this block. Until then users configure assignments through the runtime protocol only.
- Not verified by the study: same-version window pixels, the GPUI popup behaviour for the three selects, Escape while a popup is open, the real model catalogue, and OS-menu entry paths.

### Terminal scrollbar × IME real-window acceptance (N02ap/N02aq, 2026-11-20)

- Evidence `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/scrollbar-ime-02/` (notes.md, result.json, 112 PNGs, raw event logs) compares the isolated native window with the isolated reference `MatouReference-924de28d.app` under identical conditions (2,500 numbered lines, 1200×780 pt at 2×, Sogou Pinyin, real CGEvent drags, per-frame OCR of row numbers and thumb pixels). Fade timing (still visible ≈1.4 s after release, hidden within ≈100 ms after leaving the card, restored ≈150 ms after return), thumb size 6×40 px and colour (1/255 blue difference) match.
- Confirmed differences: (1) holding the thumb and typing — plain key or Pinyin — leaves the native view in history with no inline preedit, while the reference returns to the live prompt on the first letter and keeps the drag; (2) a wheel event while the thumb is held is ignored by native (reference scrolls 12 lines / 6 px); (3) during fast drags the native thumb moves one sampled step (~160 ms) before the text follows, where the reference updates in the same frame; (4) after minimize+restore native ends the old drag while the reference continues it. Cross-application focus switching during a held drag was not measurable (frontmost PID did not change in the recorded attempts).
- A fix lane (`matou-scrollbar-held-01`) is implementing return-to-live on held input (plain and IME), held-drag wheel handling, content-follow latency (root cause first: IPC round trip vs request queueing) and, if feasible, gesture continuation after restore; the same probe is re-run into `scrollbar-ime-03/`. Root will adjudicate any architectural delay rather than accepting it silently.

### Scrollbar held-drag fixes (N02ap/N02aq follow-up, 2026-11-20)

- Real-window evidence `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/scrollbar-ime-03/` (53 main PNGs, plans, event logs, `result.json`, `trace-timing.json`) against the same reference data as `scrollbar-ime-02`. Fixed and verified with the same probe: typing any key or Pinyin while holding the thumb now returns to the live prompt and shows inline preedit, and the drag continues from its original base (root cause: the pinned GPUI build replays the stationary drag event every ~16 ms, re-applying the absolute drag row right after the return-to-live scroll; the fix commits a drag only when the pointer actually moved). The six-step drag now paints the thumb and the matching content in the same frame (old lead 15.72–25.21 ms; fixed request→matching draw median 21.93 ms, verified per-frame against the reference). A wheel burst while the thumb is held now scrolls again (canvas no longer consumes it; the terminal handles vertical wheel in capture while a held drag exists). Minimize/restore now continues the original gesture when Quartz reports the left button still held with the same release counter, and ends it when the button was released while hidden.
- Remaining/open: at the original ~150 ms sampling window the held-drag wheel burst reaches +10/+11/+10 lines (thumbs +4/+6/+4 px) versus the reference +12/+6; at 225 ms the same bursts reach +13/+14. The reference updates its viewport inside the renderer while native waits for the runtime frame (measured request→frame 16.28–24.98 ms), and the frozen reference evidence cannot separate OS wheel dispatch from that delay; the end-position difference of 1–2 lines is recorded, not claimed as parity. Two smaller differences remain recorded: thumb visibility in the release frame after restore and a 1-line difference on the first re-drag after restore. Cross-application focus switching during a held drag and Windows are unverified.
- Observed IME composition styling difference (next node): the reference renders composition through xterm's `.composition-view` — solid `#000` background with `#FFF` text, absolutely positioned, nowrap — while native paints underlined terminal-coloured text. Paired screenshot `ime-detail-review.png` in the same evidence directory.
- Test hygiene: the full core+runtime suite passed on reruns; under concurrent GUI acceptance load a load-sensitive `session_contract` test failed occasionally (each passed alone 5/5), and the previously recorded permission-respawn flake appeared once. Not treated as a product regression; hardening queued.

### IME composition overlay (N02aq follow-up, 2026-11-20)

- Evidence `artifacts/native-parity/native/evidence/ime-composition-01/` (notes.md, measurements.json, paired PNGs, overlay-pixel-comparison.png). Frozen rules cited from the locked `@xterm/xterm@6.0.0`: `.composition-view` is a solid `#000` / `#FFF`, absolutely positioned, nowrap, no padding; `CompositionHelper` places it at `min(buffer.x, cols-1)` × cell width and `buffer.y` × cell height, one-cell high, terminal font, and keeps the previous CSS position when the cursor leaves the viewport.
- Native now paints the same overlay instead of underlined text: active-screen cell anchor (`composition_cell`), shrink-to-fit natural line width, one cell height, solid black background, white glyphs, clipped to the grid; theme-independent.
- Real-window paired measurements: box top-left, size (79×28 physical px at 2×), pure-black/white values, light/dark invariance, scrollback and released positions are identical to the reference; Esc cancels with nothing left on the prompt, Space commits 「你好」, Return commits `nihao` without executing, candidate window geometry unchanged, and the previous scrollbar behaviours (held input return-to-live, held wheel, six-step same-frame drag, minimize continuation) were spot-checked without regression.
- **User decision (2026-11-20): this overlay is accepted on the "not visually indistinguishable" bar** (same as the recovery water): geometry, position, size, colour and anchor must be exact (they are); glyph rasterisation may differ. Raw data kept: 461/2212 pixels differ inside the box, text bottom 1 physical pixel lower, consistently in light and dark; the difference comes from GPUI vs Chromium text rasterisation, not position or colour. Not yet paired: long composition at the right edge and composition with an existing selection.

### Authorization update (user, 2026-11-20)

- The user authorized (a) native writes to `provider-config.json` and (b) enabling the request gateway and subscription services, and asked to proceed in parallel per the 275-item porting inventory (`docs/native-parity/PORTING-MAP-2026-11-20.md`, evidence `porting-map-01/`).
- Standing hard constraints still apply: never merge to `main`, never publish/replace the installed app, never modify real user data, no ad-hoc pixel tolerances or masks, the reference product is read-only outside isolation. Provider-config work must keep the frozen file format and normalization, write atomically, preserve unknown fields, keep credentials out of logs/evidence, operate only on isolated data roots in tests, and back up before the first schema-affecting write. Subscription sign-in and the request gateway are sequenced (gateway first; subscription after its own gate), not landed by this authorization alone.

### Porting-map batch B01 — first contract gates (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-schema`: 27 fixed cases run against both implementations (87 schema objects, migration gate, transaction rollback, receipt dedup/replay). 25 match; T12/T13 differed only in JSON object key order — frozen JS enumerates integer-index keys ascending (`1,2,10,01`), native preserved insertion order (`10,2,01,1`). Fixed by a shared recursive JS key-order pass at the event write, commit result/receipt and replay-read boundaries (`core/src/storage/transaction.rs`); T12/T13 payload, receipt and replay JSON are now byte-identical (61/71/71 bytes, first differing offset 14 before, none after) and 118 core tests pass. Fix evidence `gates/K-storage-schema-keyorder-01/`. `M09` differs only by the frozen migration report carrying `backupPath` (documented interface boundary); error wrapper types differ while codes/messages match. Evidence `porting-map-01/gates/K-storage-schema/`.

The base follows the workspace theme (`#fbfcff` quantised from the frozen `#fafcff`), not terminal OSC colours, so the earlier "dark terminal showed light water" case is a test-conditions mismatch; dark water stays deferred until an app-theme switch exists.

Differences: U+007F printed by native but skipped by frozen; DECRQSS `m` replies actual SGR instead of frozen `0m`; a cancelled DCS still emits the pending reply; invalid UTF-8 inserts U+FFFD instead of skipping bytes; `CSI 6n` reports the scroll-region-relative row while frozen reports the absolute row; mode 47 re-entry keeps old alternate content instead of clearing; SGR 59 exports default instead of explicit white; invalid DECSCUSR 9 does not toggle blink. All have recorded minimal fixes; none fixed yet. Evidence `gates/K-terminal-paint/`.

Desktop entry always passes explicit paths, so the user-visible part is the read-only/recovery entry (a separate P0 page), not the CLI defaults. Evidence `gates/K-bootstrap-first/`.

The launch projection now also writes the budget, the 90% auto-compact threshold and the 1M switch after clearing stale values.

### Porting-map batch B05 — terminal selection gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-terminal-selection`: 32 fixed cases / 175 observations against the frozen `@xterm/xterm 6.0.0` selection service, replayed through the native `Terminal`/`SelectionAction`. 162 observations equal: bidirectional drag, line-end, soft wrap, wide/CJK/emoji boundaries, four-way column selection, blank/NBSP columns, double-click word and triple-click line, shift extension, programmatic set/clear, viewport scroll, drag autoscroll, column-only reflow, CSI 2J/3J clears, RIS and alternate-screen round trips. Re-running the gate reproduced `inputs.json`, `frozen.json`, `native.json`, `differences.json` and `result.md` byte-identically, and the four existing export fixtures are unchanged.
- 13 differing observations reduce to four defects, none fixed yet: (1) no native select-all entry point — `SelectionAction` and the desktop terminal lack it, so the frozen `0 → end of active buffer` range and its extension on later output are absent; (2) a row-count change keeps the old native selection, while the frozen selection service clears on `rowsChanged` (column-only resizes already agree); (3) programmatic clear drops the column mode, so the next `setSelection` copies by row instead of by column (`def` vs `ghijk`); (4) history trimming evicts whole pages in native but rows in frozen, which shifts absolute selection rows and the viewport origin for tiny capacities (the surviving word still stays selected in both). Minimal fixes are recorded per observation in the gate report.
- Not covered here: real pointer press/move/release, double/triple-click timing, Shift/Option modifiers, pixel hit-testing, capture outside the window, the ~50 ms edge-scroll schedule, selection highlight, keyboard shortcuts, system clipboard and window resize/component replay. Evidence `gates/K-terminal-selection/` (gitignored); the only tracked change is the capture test `apps/native/terminal/tests/selection_state_contract.rs`.

### Porting-map batch B06 — session storage gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-session`: 73 cases (32 session/run, 28 graph, 13 interaction) over the same fixed inputs against the frozen runtime at `924de28d` and the native core. All 32 session/run cases and all transaction, event, receipt and error rows are equal, including replay dedupe/conflict, run ordinals and generations, archive guards, concurrent-writer rollback and reopen. The gate re-runs from `run.sh` and its three native captures are byte-identical between an `--include-ignored` pass and a repeat pass.
- Four difference groups remain, none fixed yet. D1: the native projection omits `sharedSessionCount`, so `sharedWorkingDirectory` is computed from canvas membership instead of every unarchived session with the same cwd in the scene's workspace (G27/G28, and the same values embedded in work-status and interaction events); native also carries `permissionMode`/`model` on nodes where the frozen graph has neither. D2: fork acceptance returns a smaller payload, writes a `provider_bindings` row the frozen has and a different relation-event set, skews the accept receipt's `last_event_seq`, records the child as `starting` where the frozen row is `idle`, and writes an extra replay receipt when a new command reuses a `submissionKey`. D3: history load leaves `restoreState=none`/`validatedAt=null`/`canFork=false` where frozen has `restoring`/`3300`/`true`, and skips the frozen `agent.notification` that withdraws the restore reminder; its `session.mode-changed` event carries `workspace_id=null`. D4: loading history into a card with a live run is rejected at the storage entry instead of replacing the identity, and the archived rejection uses a different visible copy. Field-level locations and minimal fixes are in the report.
- The report also notes native `Terminal`-independent limits: closed-DB reopen is a SQLite file reopen, not a window reopen, and real GUI ordering, shared-directory hints, fork progress, load prompts and PTY timing stay for the G-level gates. Evidence `gates/K-storage-session/` (gitignored); tracked changes are the three capture tests and the three session contract exporters.

### G-request-gateway phase 1 — per-run loopback gateway for API-key role targets (2026-11-20)

- A fresh Claude launch that resolves role assignments fully inside API-key providers now starts a private gateway before the PTY: `127.0.0.1:0`, one random 32-byte URL prefix per run, a dedicated current-thread Tokio runtime outside the host loop. The CLI sees `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, a placeholder API key, `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS=1`, `matou-role/<role>` tier defaults (plus Fable→Opus) and the frozen `modelOverrides`, in both the process environment and the run's private `--settings` env. Real provider keys stay in the runtime snapshot and never reach the CLI or the settings file.
- Each `POST /v1/messages` / `count_tokens` resolves the requested model (role alias, `[1m]` suffix, source tier IDs, literal fallback), rewrites the upstream model, applies the frozen effort/thinking/`max_tokens` rules for messages only, strips caller auth, cookies and hop-by-hop headers, merges `anthropic-beta`, and sends exactly one request over a fresh HTTP/1.1 connection — no redirect following, decompression, retry or pooling. 3xx with `Location` becomes a fixed 502. SSE and plain bodies stream through with downstream backpressure; cancel, retire, exit and runtime close abort the outbound exchange and close the listener so the old URL is refused. 64 MiB bounds declared length, received bytes and the rewritten body.
- Unassigned launches, subscription/OAuth or keyless mixed setups, and any environment with a proxy that an external target might need keep the existing direct launch. A saved assignment does not reroute a live card; cold recovery, permission restarts and Fork keep their bound provider and rebuild nothing yet (phase 2).
- Verification I reproduced: gateway unit tests 5/5 (routing/two keys, beta merge + no follow/replay, SSE first chunk + backpressure, cancel before/after headers, 64 MiB), runtime `process_contract` 34/34 (adds the assigned-gateway process test with fake upstreams and secret-leak assertions), `provider_launch_contract` 24/24, `cargo fmt --check`, `git diff --check`, `pnpm check:identifiers`. Real CLI probe (`probe-claude-request-gateway.py`, CLI 2.1.329, isolated HOME, two loopback fakes): same PID/run/generation across `/model sonnet`, both requests carry the same model ID, each lane sees only its own key, both replies visible, recorded in `tooling/native-parity/gateway-phase-1-cli-evidence.json`.
- Not verified: phase 2 (live assignment changes, cold-recovery/fork/permission gateways, catalog probing, production HTTPS/proxy, Windows, third-party providers, general streaming pass-through and malformed request targets), phase 3 (web search, custom compaction), phase 4 (official OAuth/subscription, revocation, the single controlled retry). DeepSeek inline-tool compatibility conversion is still absent on both the direct and gateway paths — a pre-existing gap, unchanged by this slice.

### Terminal selection defects — select-all, resize and column-mode retention (2026-11-20)

- Fixed three of the four `K-terminal-selection` defects and re-ran the gate myself: 13 differing observations drop to 6, and the six survivors are all the deferred page-vs-row history-trim cases. `programmatic-after-column`, all three `select-all-*` observations, `resize-rows/*` and `resize-both/*` now match; `run.sh --verify` reproduced every gate artefact byte-identically.
- `SelectionAction::SelectAll` resolves its range from the live active buffer (`0,0 → last absolute row, cols`) so later output extends it, keeps a column mode if one was active, and is cancelled by a new mouse selection — matching `SelectionModel.finalSelectionStart/finalSelectionEnd` and `SelectionService.selectAll`. Desktop `Cmd+A` (mac only, no other modifier) emits it in the same layer as copy/paste; on other platforms `Ctrl+A` still reaches the PTY as `^A`, matching `Keyboard.ts:352-356`.
- A successful resize that changes rows clears the range and keeps the mode (`SelectionService.ts:158-161`); column-only resizes leave the selection alone. Programmatic `Clear` keeps the column mode (`SelectionService.ts:267-271`), so the next `setSelection` still copies by column.
- Known library-level divergence, not user-visible and not fixed: frozen never resets `_activeSelectionMode` on clear, buffer activation, RIS or trim, while native resets it to character on alternate-screen switches, hard resets and whole-selection trims. The next mouse press re-decides the mode in both engines, and the gate's cases cannot observe the difference. Terminal crate tests 43 pass; desktop tests 32 pass; fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` pass. Real-window Cmd+A, highlight, window resize and the disabled-input clipboard path are still unverified.

### Session graph D1 — workspace-wide shared-directory counts (2026-11-20)

- The graph projection now counts every unarchived session with the same cwd in the scene's task workspace (not just canvas members), emits `sharedSessionCount = max(1, count)`, and derives `sharedWorkingDirectory` and `worktree.shared` from `count > 1` — the same query as the frozen `cwdUseCounts`. The field also lands in work-status events, interaction events and receipts because they embed the same projection.
- I re-ran the gate myself: the 431 `sharedSessionCount` rows and the one shared-flag row are gone. What remains is D2 (fork acceptance shape/events/receipt), D3 (history-load restore state and notification) and D4 (live-run and archived load rejections), plus the native-only `permissionMode`/`model` node fields that the desktop still reads — kept on purpose and recorded.
- The four pinned graph fixtures were updated for these two fields only; I stripped them out and diffed the parsed JSON against the previous fixtures to confirm no other value changed, and the frozen gate independently produced the same counts (2 shared across canvases, 1 after archiving the sibling). Core tests 139 pass; fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` pass. The desktop shared-directory hint has not been checked in a real window.

### Porting-map batch B07 — search policy gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-search-policy`: 149 fixed cases (55 terminal-buffer, 22 terminal-session, 72 archived-history) against the frozen search addon and archive reader. Six raw field differences in two classes only; everything else matches, including case/regex/whole-word flags, CJK and emoji boundaries, match ordering, decorations/counts, the `0/0` copy, clean/alternate-screen states, missing segments, CRC-corrupt segments, the gzip fallback and empty queries' result sets.
- Differences: (1) an empty query reports `resultIndex = 0` in frozen and `-1` in native while both have zero matches and no active item — the desktop renders the same "no match" state, and the fix is a boundary-only change reserved for the next terminal slice; (2) an invalid regular expression rejects on both sides, but the frozen text appends the engine's own V8 message while native returns the stable `invalid terminal search pattern` prefix. I am keeping the native message on purpose: copying V8's engine text would pin us to an engine string, and the desktop already maps the class to the same 「正则表达式无效」 copy (approved deviation D02).
- Not covered: the real search bar, highlight and overview-ruler painting, keyboard shortcuts, focus, reconnect, timed refreshes from background output, the live→archive switch, D03's retained context after zero results, late replies and Esc back to the terminal. Evidence `gates/K-search-policy/` (gitignored); tracked changes are the two capture tests and the two exporters.

### History-trim divergence — accepted (2026-11-20)

- The six surviving `K-terminal-selection` observations are one cause: the frozen selection service follows xterm's row-by-row scrollback trim, while the native terminal's scrollback is a page list inside the vendored VT library and evicts whole pages. At the configured 10,000 lines the native buffer keeps about 166 fewer history rows at the eviction boundary; the selected word survives in both and the coordinates follow each side's own actual trim.
- Decision: do not patch the vendored page allocator for this. The difference is bounded by one page, invisible for normal scrolling, and rewriting a third-party allocator to match xterm's granularity would put the whole terminal surface at risk for no user-visible gain. Recorded here and in the gate report; the empty-query `resultIndex` difference from `K-search-policy` is likewise queued as a boundary-only cleanup, not a behaviour fix.

### Porting-map batch B08 — journal storage gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-journal`: 53 fixed cases across codec, segments and history reading — frame encoding and per-frame CRC, truncations inside a frame head/body, zero-byte and torn records, gzip valid/invalid-header/truncated-header/truncated-trailer/torn-record inputs, multi-segment publication, cross-segment pagination, missing and corrupt gaps, UTF-8 split across raw and gzip segments, and archive history paging with anchors/limits.
- Everything matches except four fields in three cases plus one physical-byte case. (1) gzip rejection classes differ in wording only (frozen surfaces the zlib codes `Z_DATA_ERROR`/`Z_BUF_ERROR`, native surfaces `invalid gzip header` and `UnexpectedEof`), and both reject the same inputs and deliver the same already-decoded frames first. (2) A truncated gzip header is misclassified by native as `invalid Journal V2 magic` instead of an EOF — a diagnostics-only wording issue queued for a small cleanup; no reader changes. (3) The gzip container of the same 411-byte segment is 249 B when frozen writes it and 246 B when native writes it, first differing at byte 9 — different compressors; segment names, decompressed bytes, CRCs and read order are identical, so the acceptance is on decompressed bytes. I am not aligning compressor output.
- Not covered: the desktop history-gap UI and scroll/return to live, IPC argument coercion, concurrent compression/publication races, power loss and real device IO faults. Evidence `gates/K-storage-journal/` (gitignored); tracked changes are the two capture tests and the exporter's new cases.

### Porting-map batch B09 — terminal mouse gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-terminal-mouse`: 358 probes across tracking modes (X10/1000/1002/1003), encodings (default/1005/1006/1015/1016), modifiers, press/release/move/wheel, CJK and wide-character columns, boundary cells, mode stacking and 1003 bursts. Encoder bytes, isolated-PTY byte read-back and selection state match everywhere except one comparable field: the blank cell after a CJK wide character yields `""` in frozen and `" "` in native; both reject it as selection content, so no user-visible difference.
- Recorded shared behaviour: both applications generate default-encoding reports but filter them at the application layer, so programs relying on the legacy encoding receive nothing on either side. Making the legacy encoding reach the PTY would be a product change on both sides, not a parity fix.
- Not covered: real mouse events, pixel hit-testing, selection highlight and the full desktop→PTY path (G-level). Evidence `gates/K-terminal-mouse/` (gitignored); the only tracked change is the capture test.

### Porting-map batch B10 — storage backup gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-backup`: 10 fixed cases over clean-exit backup, uncheckpointed WAL, seven-file rotation, sidecar manifest content/permissions, integrity failure, target replacement, a 250 ms copy budget, directory-conflict rollback and publication failures, plus an unwritable directory. Both sides keep the newest seven backups, refuse corrupted indexes, preserve the previous backup on every failure, and publish a `0600` manifest whose size and self-hash verify.
- Field differences are the file sets and per-file hashes: native publishes the database on its own (no empty `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars), so ordering, headers and manifest hashes differ while contents verify. OS error text differs (`EISDIR … rename` vs `Is a directory (os error 21)`); the timeout case injects at different points — frozen defers the copy result and can still publish after the wait is released, native stops on the exhausted budget and cleans its staging files, and both check cancellation before publishing. Queued as small storage-diagnostics cleanups: a phase-qualified rename error, reclaiming an orphan database when manifest publication fails, and classifying a truncated gzip header as EOF instead of journal magic.
- Not covered: a real 294 MB copy, real quit/force-quit, sleep/wake during a copy and a genuinely full disk. Evidence `gates/K-storage-backup/` (gitignored); tracked changes are the two contract tests and the exporter's cases.

### Claude model catalog — offline CLI probe in the runtime (2026-11-20)

- New runtime module mirrors the frozen `nativeClaudeModels()`: it resolves the same CLI executable as a launch (including the existing login-shell fallback), runs it in a temporary config directory with inherited `ANTHROPIC_`/`CLAUDE_`/`MATOU_` variables stripped and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:1`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=LOCAL_METADATA_ONLY` and nonessential traffic disabled, then asks the control protocol for `initialize`, `set_model` and `get_context_usage` (base and `[1m]`) to produce id, resolved id, display name, context window, maximum window and supported efforts. Aliases (`default`/`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`/`fable`) are kept alongside their resolved ids, results are deduped the same way, a six-second deadline kills and reaps the child, and every failure returns an empty list.
- The host answers `GetClaudeModelCatalog` on its own thread through the Graph reply (`claudeModelCatalogResult`) and caches the outcome, including an empty one, for 60 seconds from probe start; concurrent requests share one probe. Real CLI evidence (2.1.329, isolated HOME, network-disabled sandbox): 9 entries, `default` → `claude-opus-5-5`, 1,000,000-token base and maximum windows, all five effort levels, cold probe about 0.59 s. My own run: 3 stub-process tests pass (protocol/caching, unavailable → empty, 6.01 s timeout with the child reaped while the host still answers settings requests) and the real-CLI smoke test stays opt-in.
- Wiring the desktop assignment page to this reply is the next step; Windows, other CLI versions and the PATH-miss path are not yet measured.

### Porting-map batch B11 — storage recovery gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-recovery`: 16 fixed cases over clean exit, a real SIGKILL of a lease-holding child, a missing marker, a missing SHM, marker text corruption, an unreadable marker, a tampered database, a read-only directory, a damaged database inside a read-only directory, resolve-and-reopen and the retry action. Both sides recognise the same states, keep the original database bytes, refuse to clobber a marker they cannot publish and read back the committed WAL row after a forced exit.
- What the native product still lacks is the layer above the storage gate: the host blocks the startup (`ReadOnly`/`Configuration`) but there is no recovery page, no browsable read-only workspace and no 「重新检查数据库」 action yet; those belong to the recovery UI gates. Field differences are error text/OS codes (`STORAGE_READ_ONLY` vs SQLite 8, `EACCES` vs `Permission denied (os error 13)`) and backup file sets; the queued storage-diagnostics cleanup adds phase/path context to marker publication errors while keeping OS codes.
- Not covered: a real desktop window, desktop crashes, power loss, sleep and real read-only volumes. Evidence `gates/K-storage-recovery/` (gitignored); tracked changes are the two contract tests and the exporter's cases.

### Porting-map batch B12 — PTY derived-info gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-pty-derived`: 56 fixed cases over cwd tracking (OSC 7 and titles, percent-escapes, host/share forms, Chinese and space paths, invalid escapes, command echoes), work-status transitions across prompts/commands/output, and card summaries (ANSI stripping, CJK, emoji with ZWJ, combining marks, UTF-16 truncation at the window edge, multi-line merging/dedupe). Everything matches except two edge cases and one shared behaviour.
- Differences: (1) a legacy `file:///C|/…` URL is normalised to `/C:/…` by frozen and left as `/C|/…` by native — a Windows-only cwd form; (2) a summary window that cuts an emoji in half keeps the lone surrogate `U+DE00` in frozen while native substitutes `U+FFFD` (the UTF-8 bytes agree). Both are queued for a terminal/runtime cleanup slice along with the shared ANSI-stripping note; neither changes what a card displays today. Not covered: real shell prompts, Windows ConPTY, timed refreshes and on-card rendering.

### G-assignment-ui — three-tier model assignments in the settings page (2026-11-20)

- The Claude Code page now carries the frozen 「模型分配」 block: Opus/Sonnet/Haiku rows with provider, model and reasoning-effort selects, the 「最终／当前目标」 line, per-row validation with the frozen copy, the privacy note, 「新会话使用此分配，正在运行的会话不受影响」, 「恢复默认」 and 「保存分配」. Edits write through the existing role-assignment commands; an unconfirmed save keeps the draft and shows the frozen retry copy; the reply rebuilds the baseline from what the runtime stored.
- Rules follow the frozen helpers: an unassigned tier follows the default provider and shows the inherited effort name; a custom API provider without a catalog may save a manually typed, valid model id; providers with a catalog reject ids outside it; missing keys block with 「API Key 未配置，请先编辑供应商。」; the official built-in provider with no catalog shows the frozen catalog error until the new model-catalog request is wired in. Subscription providers are not offered (subscribed routing arrives with gateway phase 4); a previously stored subscription target stays visible as unavailable with the frozen account copy.
- Real-window evidence in an isolated app: three tiers following the default provider, Opus assigned to the second provider with effort 高, the save receipt, reopen persistence, restore-to-default, keyboard menu operation, and the missing-key rejection with save disabled; SQLite read-back shows only the Opus assignment and empty rows after restore. Desktop tests 35 pass (21 unit + 14 integration) on my own run; fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` pass.
- Pending, recorded in the matrix: the web-search row (gateway phase 3), wiring the model-catalog request into the row selects (next slice), subscription assignments (phase 4), context windows, compaction targets and dispatch rows, Windows, narrow windows, dark theme and full-frame pixel equality.

### Session storage D3 + D4 — history-load restore state, dismissal, rejections (2026-11-20)

- Loading a Claude conversation now matches the frozen provider-mode transaction: the binding is stored as `resume_state=available` / `restore_state=restoring`, `validated_at` and `metadata.spawnRevision` both take the load time, `canFork` becomes true for Claude, the previous error is cleared, other bindings are expired with a fresh `invalidated_at`, and the same transaction writes `session.mode-changed` plus an `agent.notification` dismissal (`provider-restore:<session>`) carrying the scene's workspace and task. Replaying the same command does not send a second notification. A real host+PTY process test now asserts the dismissal, the `restoring` projection and that `providerSpawnRevision` equals the notification's clock.
- A load into an archived card reports the frozen `Session does not exist` first, keeping the scene and working-directory checks behind it. A load into a card with a live run is still refused at the storage entry — the host retires the old run, waits for its persisted exit and then commits, which matches frozen once the attach step is reached. I am keeping the native order (stop, then commit): frozen commits the new binding and only then retires the old process, so a failed stop would leave frozen with a switched identity and a live old process, while native keeps the previous binding. That failure path is recorded for the G-level check rather than reworked.
- Residual differences after the fix are shape/transport, not values: the native load reply stays flat (`sessionId`/`bindingId`/`restoreState`) instead of frozen's `{session,binding,graph}`, it echoes the notification inside the reply while frozen only emits it, the G24 storage guard remains by design, and carried-forward snapshot rows keep the D2 fork-operation naming and native-only `permissionMode`/`model` fields. Core tests pass on my run; the storage gate reproduces with only those residual fields.

### Fork acceptance (D2) — deferred (2026-11-20)

- The remaining `K-storage-session` D2 rows are the fork-accept return shape (`{sessionId,stage,error,replayed}` vs frozen `{hierarchy,graph,forkState,forkProgress}`), the event set (native writes a mount, a canvas membership and `relation.created` where frozen writes `structural-relation-created`), the bindings row the frozen inherits for the child, the child's persisted `work_status` (`idle` frozen / `starting` native, both projected as `starting`), the internal operation-id form, and one extra replay receipt.
- Decision: do not chase this now. Everything a user sees — the child card, its starting state, the relation and the progress display — is produced by both, and the divergences are transport and storage naming. Rewriting the fork transaction to emit frozen-named events would churn a flow that is about to gain a real-worktree step in its own gate; it is recorded here and in the gate report instead.

### Model catalog wired into the assignment page (2026-11-20)

- The settings panel now requests `GetClaudeModelCatalog` once per panel session when the default provider (or any row's provider) is the built-in official one without `modelMetadata`, and folds the reply into that provider's model choices exactly like the frozen `availableModels()` — alias rows (`default`/`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`/`fable`, keeping their resolved id, display name, windows and effort levels) plus the concrete ids. Custom providers keep their own catalog/mapping and never see these entries; any other provider state, an empty catalog or an error keeps the frozen catalog copy and a disabled save. 「重试」 re-requests through the host, whose 60-second cache the retry deliberately respects.
- Real-window evidence with Claude Code 2.1.329 (isolated app): the official single-provider page shows `Opus`/`Sonnet`/`Haiku` defaults on all three rows with no catalog error, an assigned Opus row saves the alias `opus` (SQLite read-back), and reopening the panel keeps it. Hidden probes cover request dedupe, empty/error blocking, retry, effort levels from the catalog and custom-provider isolation. My run: desktop tests 35 pass; fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` pass.
- Not verified: Windows, other CLI versions, a real retry inside the 60-second cache window and real model calls.

### Porting-map batch B13 — shell history gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-shell-history`: 18 fixed cases over completion-OSC parsing (133/633 markers, partial markers, unknown subcommands, embedded newlines, over-long commands), the 100-entry cap and eviction of the oldest, consecutive duplicates, CRLF and control characters in the restored payload, and a SQLite close/reopen of the history store. Order, counts, eviction and the reopened payload match byte for byte.
- Two defects are shared with the reference and therefore kept as parity, not fixed: an OSC marker split between `ESC` and `]` across chunks is missed by both sides, and a very long command split before its terminator is missed by both while the same command in one chunk is stored. The single field difference is the familiar lone surrogate: when the 64 KiB window cuts an emoji, frozen keeps `U+DE42` and native writes `U+FFFD` (UTF-8 bytes and the reopened payload agree). The surrogate class joins the queued cleanup with the PTY-derived case.
- Not covered: real shell configurations emitting OSC, Windows PowerShell notifications, a real app restart and on-screen history.

### Porting-map batch B14 — storage ownership gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-owner`: 16 fixed cases over lock-file fields and order, permissions, live and dead PIDs, a simulated PID reuse, path identity (`./matou.sqlite`), corrupt/empty/unreadable locks, takeover side files, release ordering and a generation sequence of `g1 → g2 → g1` (both sides accept a non-increasing generation at the lock layer). Both sides take over stale owners, isolate by lock location and keep `0600`.
- One real defect: after a release is denied by directory permissions and the directory is restored, a second release on the same lease reports success in native but leaves `matou.sqlite.owner` behind, so the next ownership check keeps failing — frozen clears the file and the guard passes. A failed release must report its system code and target path, and a later successful release must actually delete the matching lock. Queued in the diagnostics-cleanup slice as the highest-priority item because it can leave the app unable to reopen after a permission mishap.
- Field-level differences are read-back shapes (native keeps unknown fields) and message text for a zero-byte lock. Real two-process contention, holder termination, NFS and Windows named locks stay with `G-storage-owner`.

### Porting-map batch B15 — task lifecycle gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-task-delete`: 60 fixed cases over pinning order and workspace scope, rename limits and duplicates, deleting empty/running tasks, the last-canvas refusal, canvas and session cascade, two injected failure points and replay/replay-conflict. Both sides release the same session ids, commit exactly one event and receipt, and roll back to byte-identical snapshots at both failure points.
- Remaining fields are already-known classes: the native-only `permissionMode` node field and the SQLite wrapper error code (`ERR_SQLITE_ERROR` vs the native code). No behavioural difference; real drag reordering, board drag-and-drop, the delete confirmation dialog and real process exits stay with the G-level gates.

### Porting-map batch B16 — terminal scroll gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-terminal-scroll`: 32 cases, 210 geometry probes and three ordered frames against the frozen scrollbar and viewport. Track geometry, thumb size/top, click/drag mapping, absolute positioning, live-growth following and the production-limit anchoring start out identical; the geometry fixtures and the ordered-frame replay are reproducible byte for byte.
- User-visible differences recorded for the next slice: (1) wheel scrolling over the viewport accumulates pixels in frozen (29/11/11/12/12) but snaps in native (26/25/25/25/25/26); (2) Shift+PageUp/PageDown scrolls by `rows-1` in frozen while native forwards the key encoding to the PTY instead of scrolling; (3) with history mid-view, a resize anchors the viewport differently (frozen keeps the requested row after growth, native jumps to it earlier); (4) per-entry scrollbar insets differ (frozen 14 / 12 / 4 for default / canvas / hierarchy, native 12 everywhere); (5) the history-trim cases inherit the accepted page-granular eviction, plus a browse-state difference after `CSI 3J`. The empty value vs computed value in the hidden state is a harness, not a product, difference.
- Real pointers, trackpad normalization, held-thumb replay, pixel hit-testing and production frame timing stay with the G-level gate.

### Scroll behaviour fixes — wheel granularity, Shift paging, resize anchor, insets (2026-11-20)

- Four user-visible `K-terminal-scroll` differences are fixed: wheel events over the viewport now use the same pixel accumulation as the track (an event is 50 px, a precise pixel delta keeps its 50/40 scale, Alt multiplies by five, rounding up), Shift+PageUp/PageDown scroll locally by `rows − 1` before any key encoding is sent, a resize converts the browsing anchor to the new row coordinates while keeping the track pixel anchor separate, and the surface takes a per-entry vertical inset (14 px default, 12 px in the canvas; the native app has no hierarchy terminal panel, so that entry is not applicable).
- To keep desktop pixel positioning and the host's row positioning from compensating twice, `ScrollTo` gains a default-off `pixel_target` flag that the host/worker only forward; the worker still coalesces adjacent destinations of the same kind, and the ordered frame ids are unchanged. A read-only `matou_vt_scrollbar` shim query returns offset/total/length without rebuilding the grid; an lldb count over two resizes shows `matou_vt_resize` ×2 against `matou_vt_scrollbar` ×8 with no extra screen reads.
- Verification I reproduced: the gate's `--verify` run is byte-identical and the five target cases drop from 56 differing fields to 0 (the whole gate keeps only the accepted history-trim, `CSI 3J` browsing and hidden-state harness fields); terminal 4 suites and desktop 5 suites pass; real isolated window: wheel `58 → 55 → 51`, Shift+PageUp 42 lines, a 43→32-row shrink and back restores the same visible range. Physical trackpad pixel events and animation continuity remain unverified.

### Contract-gate diagnostic cleanups (2026-11-20)

- Six small fixes from the recent gates: (1) `DatabaseOwner::release` now removes the matching lock before marking the handle released, so a release denied by permissions can be retried and the second attempt really deletes the file (the `release-denied-retry` guard now passes; the remaining fields are Node's `EACCES` message/code versus the native text); the release error carries the lock path. (2) A truncated gzip journal header is classified as an end-of-file corruption instead of `invalid Journal V2 magic`, keeping the decoder cause. (3) Backup publishing refuses to overwrite a same-name backup, and a manifest-publication failure reclaims the just-published, unpaired database and its empty side files while leaving the old backup, source database and WAL byte-identical — this is why the backup gate now records one extra file-presence row where frozen leaves the orphan. (4) Rename failures in backup publication and rollback carry the stage and relative target while keeping the OS code. (5) An empty search query reports `resultIndex = 0`; a non-empty query with no match stays `-1`. (6) A legacy `file:///C|/…` URL normalises the drive letter to `C:` and leaves `%7C` and ordinary paths untouched.
- I re-ran all five affected gates myself: all exit 0, the search gate drops 6 → 4 differing probes, the owner guard case is functionally closed, and the journal/derived counts are unchanged or lower with only diagnostic text or the accepted lone-surrogate case left. Kept as before: the lone surrogate at a UTF-16 window edge and the OSC/PowerShell splits shared with the reference. Desktop surfaces and real user databases were not part of this slice.

### Porting-map batch B17 — storage durability gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-storage-durability`: 22 fixed cases over watermark progression and re-reads (equal, behind, ahead), retention-byte boundaries (exact, over, zero), eviction afterwards, retry ordering after injected write failures, and publication interrupted before/after the rename and directory sync. Both sides read back complete frames in every case.
- Two recovery-detail differences: after a failed rename frozen leaves `.partial` while native removes it; after a successful rename whose directory sync fails, frozen keeps the old segment plus the archive on reopen while native retires this archive and republishes — both converge on complete frames. Native's cleanup is the tidier behaviour and is kept; recorded for the G-level durability gate with real crash timing. Not covered: real power loss, system IO faults, a real 294 MB rotation, on-screen notices and cross-card isolation.

### G-request-gateway phase 2 — resumed, forked and restarted runs get their own gateway (2026-11-20)

- Cold resume, Fork and permission restart now prepare the same per-run gateway before the PTY starts, reading the assignment preferences at that moment: resume and permission restart keep the bound conversation identity and restored model, Fork keeps the parent `--resume --fork-session` identity while getting an independent child address, and a running parent keeps its own address until it retires. Retirement closes the listener before returning, so an old URL is refused as soon as the new run starts. Only-effort API-key sessions (tier or global effort without assignments) also get a gateway, projected through the frozen `matou-api/<role>` aliases, and rewrite `output_config.effort` per message while counts stay untouched.
- General requests (anything except the two JSON entry points) now stream through with a 64 MiB bound instead of buffering: a 1 MiB `POST /v1/models` visibly reaches the upstream in two arrivals, a chunked upload is cut off and answered 413 right past 64 MiB, methods/queries are preserved and inbound trailers are dropped. The JSON entry points keep buffering and rewriting.
- Verification I reproduced: gateway goldens 8/8 (routing, effort-only, general streaming + 64 MiB abort, beta/no-follow, SSE, 64 MiB bounds), gateway process tests 2/2 (fresh switch and the new cold-resume/Fork/permission-restart rebuild), `provider_launch_contract` 24/24, fmt and `git diff --check`. The lane's real-CLI run (2.1.329, isolated HOME) resumed the same `providerSessionId` and switched from upstream A to B across the restart with matching synthetic-key fingerprints; Fork and permission restart are covered by real runtime processes with a substitute CLI.
- Out of scope, unchanged: live hot switching (frozen keeps the launch snapshot), subscriptions/OAuth/keyless official, search, custom compaction, DeepSeek inline-tool conversion, proxy egress, Windows, and production upstreams for Fork/permission paths.

### Porting-map batch B18 — canvas motion gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-canvas-motion`: 11 fixed timelines plus 4 easing curves sampled at 16 ms with extra samples at the endpoint and around edge confirmation. Final widths and lifts match; easing differs only below 1e-6, which is solver/serialisation noise.
- Two visible timing differences recorded for a follow-up fix: (1) after an edge confirmation the new width target starts one sample later in native (196 → 197 ms) and growth begins at 198 ms — the frozen order handles the due edge preview before the width-target loop; (2) when the window width crosses a rule change the native card jumps its width in one frame (e.g. 176 → 361 px at 816 ms) while frozen keeps the Width curve and transitions `flex-basis` from the old-rule width. Real frame rate, visuals, pointer edge cruising, focus and character landing stay with `G-canvas-motion`.

### Porting-map batch B19 — archived history selection gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-history-selection`: 16 archived-page cases plus 5 auto-scroll curves replayed on both sides (frozen runs the page in an Electron/Chromium hidden page, native uses the production `HistoryView` and GPUI layout). Curves match frame by frame, cross-blank-row selections and texts match, and the page/footer geometry matches.
- Differences: (1) the dominant group is sub-pixel rounding of the measured viewport height (158.90625 vs 159) and two 0.5 px anchors (404 vs 403.5, 237.5 vs 238) — harness-level, not visible; (2) a triple-click on the last content line selects through the footer in frozen (text ends with a newline, end anchor on the footer) while native stops at the row end, so copying that line loses the trailing newline; (3) loading a page and an explicit refresh after appending clear a selection that frozen keeps. The selection gaps are queued as a small history fix; real drag/stop-on-release, selection styling and the system clipboard stay with the G-level gate.

### Provider delete in the settings editor (2026-11-20)

- The provider editor now shows 「删除」 only for a saved, non-built-in provider that is not the active one, exactly like the frozen condition. Clicking it opens an inline confirmation with the frozen copy (「删除「<name>」？」 / 「此操作移除保存的配置，不会注销账户。」 / 「继续编辑」 / 「确认删除」); cancelling returns focus to the delete button and keeps the draft. While the request runs the action buttons are disabled; success closes the dialog, refreshes the list and counts from the runtime snapshot and shows 「供应商已删除」; a rejection shows 「删除失败」 inside the dialog, keeps the draft and allows an immediate retry, after which focus moves to 「新增供应商」 because the card is gone.
- Verified with hidden GPUI probes (the real desktop was in use, so no window was taken): the three visibility cases, cancel, duplicate input during the wait, backend rejection and retry, configuration count 3 → 2 and a reopen that re-reads disk without the deleted provider. Desktop tests 37 pass on my run; fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` pass. A real-window screenshot is deferred to an idle window; the failure is surfaced inside the dialog rather than as a toast, which matches this page's existing save-failure presentation.

### History selection fixes — last-line triple-click, retained selections (2026-11-20)

- Triple-clicking the last content line now selects through the footer like frozen: the copied text keeps its trailing newline and the end anchor sits at the start of the footer (or of the 「下方还有更新记录」 hint when it exists). A footer selection survives a `loading → ready` cycle, and a body selection survives appending ten lines plus an explicit refresh — the remap keys body rows by their archive cursor (so the selection follows a row whose display index moved) and footer labels by role. I re-ran the gate myself: the eight targeted fields are gone (94 → 86 entries), and what remains is the sub-pixel height/anchor/scroll rounding plus the empty-selection endpoint representation.
- Gate artefacts reproduce byte-identically. Real drag, stop-on-release, selection styling and the system clipboard remain with the G-level gate.

### Canvas motion fixes — edge retarget and rule-change transitions (2026-11-20)

- A due edge-preview confirmation is now handled before the width-target loop, so the confirmation and the retarget land in the same render cycle (196 ms) and the card starts widening at 197 ms, matching frozen. A viewport change that crosses a column/narrow rule now animates from the old rule's current width to the new rule's width along the existing Width curve, while same-rule percentage widths keep following the viewport immediately; both ends of the animation are evaluated against the current viewport so consecutive resizes continue from the in-flight width.
- The layout rule and width rule are now explicit types (`CarouselLayout`, `WidthRule`) instead of a private normalised width; the `session_levels_probe` assertion was updated to check the final rule is expanded, without keeping a dead interface.
- Verification I reproduced: the gate's `--verify` is byte-identical with the edge group (26) and rule-change group (192) at 0 and only the eight sub-1e-6 easing samples left; the gate's frozen inputs and bundled output are unchanged from before the fix. Desktop tests pass (23 lib plus the contracts and the probe). Real frames and visuals stay with `G-canvas-motion`.

### Porting-map batch B20 — canvas tab gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-tab-close`: 24 fixed cases over canvas naming and order, rename, closing the current or a background tab, focus inheritance, the last-tab protection and two database reopen passes. Names, order, focus and close receipts persist identically; the only field differences are the known native-only `permissionMode` node field on five graph snapshots. Real close-confirmation dialogs, process exit, app restart and drag reordering stay with the G-level gate.

### Porting-map batch B21 — canvas anchor gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-canvas-anchor`: 36 fixed cases over canvas level and parent chains, switching into and out of child levels, scroll-anchor restoration, anchor persistence across reopen and resize, retry of anchor writes and priority focus with fallbacks. Levels, focus, events and errors match.
- Two recorded differences: (1) before the anchor card is mounted frozen computes an anchor of 0 while native computes 318 — both agree at 318 once mounted — native starts at the saved 318 while frozen recomputes 0 until the card mounts, which looks like a restore flash in frozen, so I keep native's value and leave the judgement to real-frame evidence at `G-canvas-anchor`; (2) the A31–A33 retry rows differ only in the recorded clock (frozen logs send time, native logs sample time) with identical retry counts and final anchors. Real scroll gestures, live window resizes, focus rings, app restart and cross-window behaviour stay with the G-level gate.

### G-storage-recovery slice 1 — recovery page and 「重新检查数据库」 (2026-11-20)

- When the startup storage check finds an ownership, WAL or integrity problem, the desktop now shows the independent recovery page instead of a blocked workspace: frozen eyebrow/title/description per reason, up to seven verified backups newest-first (metadata only, no entity or marker paths), the progress line and both startup and action errors. The page is a pure projection of the runtime's `DatabaseRecoveryRequired` details, and only 「重新检查数据库」 is offered this slice — the restore/export/start-empty actions and the read-only workspace are the next two slices, recorded in `tooling/native-parity/storage-recovery-01-MATRIX.md`.
- A re-check after repairing the database in place succeeds inside the same runtime child process and hands the normal workspace projection back once; failures keep the page and show the staged error. Both the desktop and the host reject duplicate actions, business writes are refused while recovery is pending (`STORAGE_READ_ONLY`), and a reconnect does not re-send an unfinished action. The private parent-child protocol moves to v2, with an older child rejected as a Protocol block.
- Verification I reproduced: the three recovery process cases pass (damaged database retry keeps evidence and reaches ready in the same process; a new live owner is refused and malformed evidence archived; an invalid marker and a permission-only read-only database keep the existing startup paths) plus the supervisor contract 4/4. The lane's isolated K samples cover wrong recovery IDs, marker changes, settlement conflicts and disconnect; hidden GPUI probes cover the three reason texts, backup ordering/empty state, progress and both error kinds. Foreground first-frame, real scroll/focus and a real mouse click are still unverified.

### K-dag-fixture — frozen graph fixtures delivered (2026-11-20)

- Sixteen anonymised, reproducible cases now pin the frozen session-graph behaviour for later native wiring: layout coordinates for up to 10,000 nodes (plus a 160,000-node failure sample), search matches and navigation, aggregate counts, event ordering and reconnect, and bad-seed/missing-relation/cyclic/over-deep failures. Each case carries fixed input, frozen output, boundary notes and source line references, with UUIDs, generations and clocks normalised by a documented rule.
- Fixture `apps/native/core/tests/fixtures/dag-reference.json` (SHA-256 `8b273807…`); I re-ran the exporter myself and reproduced the same hash. Two frozen edge behaviours are recorded for the implementation gate: the dropdown lists 12 entries while the keyboard can select the 13th, and reusing a window for another scene re-enters loading without re-requesting the snapshot. Native wiring, real multi-window focus, PTY output and large-graph frame performance stay with the G-level gates.

### G-storage-export slice 2 — read-only workspace, banner and bundle export (2026-11-20)

- A database that can only be opened read-only (permission-denied directory, or a schema newer than this build) now starts a read-only workspace instead of a failure: existing workspaces, tasks, canvases and sessions are listed from a read-only connection, focus switching and per-window temporary navigation stay in memory, and archived history can be paged, searched, selected and copied through the existing offline history worker (no PTY, no provider, no migration, no clean-exit backup). The live-terminal 「返回实时终端」 entry is hidden only in this mode; the online history behaviour is unchanged and its selection/copy regression is byte-identical.
- A banner under the tab strip shows the frozen copy 「数据库处于只读恢复模式」 plus the explanation, a 「搜索当前终端」 entry and 「导出数据库资料」. Export matches the frozen read-only export exactly: destination `MATOU_RECOVERY_EXPORT_DIR` or `~/Downloads/Matou-Recovery`, a `<timestamp>-<uuid>` folder holding the database and its `-wal`/`-shm` plus a `0600` `manifest.json` with `mode: read-only`, `sourceDatabasePath`, `exportedAt` and `exportedFiles`; it additionally refuses destinations inside the data root. Success/failure/in-progress copy is the frozen text.
- Desktop and host share one command-eligibility table; direct writes in the read-only stage (including bypassing the desktop) receive `STORAGE_READ_ONLY`, the private protocol moves to v3, and a reconnect does not re-send an unfinished export. I verified on both sources that the source tree is byte-identical after exit, no backup is written, and the export folder and manifest check out. Runtime tests pass serially (the earlier two intermittent PTY failures did not reproduce), core 37 suites pass, desktop suites pass, and `cargo check --all-targets`, fmt, identifier gate and `git diff --check` are clean.
- Fidelity gap recorded, not papered over: the offline view shows the raw Journal text because native has no persisted checkpointed screen body yet; frozen shows the screen-final state. Foreground window, system clipboard and standalone windows remain for an idle desktop; the remaining two write actions (restore backup, start empty) are slice 3.

### Porting-map batch B22 — recovery water contract gate (2026-11-20)

- `K-recovery-water`: 26 clock steps, four layer groups, 14 hit points, nine handoff cases and four copies compared against the frozen Canvas body (source SHA-256 recorded) and the app's static CSS executed in a hidden Chromium. Timeline, pause, fractional resize, layer geometry, colour stops, clipping and hit results match to 1e-8; the frozen SVG/Canvas draw instructions are identical to native's fluid/CoreGraphics layer functions.
- Two deterministic differences queued for a small fix: (1) after a resize during reduced motion the frozen ResizeObserver restarts the frame interval (`frameScale 0.35`) while native keeps the previous sample (`1.5`) — all draw instructions are identical, so this is a frame-sampling reset; (2) the native card header is 42 pt in this state while frozen's is 32 pt, and the water still starts at y=32, so it covers 10 pt of the header and that band hits as water/stop-bubbles/activate — to be split into “recovery-only geometry” versus a broader card-layout difference before changing anything.
- Recorded, not changed pending real-frame evidence: frozen qualifies handoff when the line text has any character even if invisible, native requires visible cells (`invisible-text`); the handoff ordering also differs (`bytes-applied…visual-ready` vs `valid-grid…sync-terminal-input`). Zero-tolerance pixel comparison, GPU/software raster differences, real crash recovery and live first-glyph handoff remain for `G-recovery-water` and the pending product decision.

### G-recovery-actions slice 3 — restore backup, start empty and the recovery-page export (2026-11-20)

- The recovery page now offers the frozen four actions. 「恢复所选备份」 re-verifies the manifest, size, SHA-256 and SQLite integrity for the chosen id, publishes a readable `.replaced-` copy when a normal database sits in place, replaces the database and clears stale WAL/SHM, and only then opens, migrates and settles into the backup's workspace; failures before replacement keep the original, post-replacement failures keep the page and the staged evidence and refresh the verified candidate list so a stale backup drops out. 「创建全新空数据库」 archives the in-place database, WAL and SHM into this request's `recovery-evidence` directory (a rename, never a delete, so a later failure leaves them retrievable), keeps the existing quarantine group, then initialises, migrates and settles a fresh workspace. 「导出恢复资料」 writes the frozen F19 bundle inside the data root (`recovery-exports/<timestamp>-<uuid>` with marker/quarantine/owner/takeover evidence and the frozen manifest), reports the real directory or the real I/O error, and leaves the recovery state pending.
- The full-backup case reproduces frozen exactly: with seven verified backups, the pre-migration backup and the clean-exit backup each rotate the oldest away in turn and seven remain valid. Two runtimes racing the same recovery cycle settle once — only one archives and settles. The desktop keeps progress until the workspace is Ready, the confirmation defaults to 返回 with round-trip Tab and Escape, focus returns to the trigger on cancel, and replies from an older generation are dropped.
- Verification I reproduced: the nine runtime recovery process cases pass (damaged-database retry, ownership retry, both read-only sources browsing/searching/exporting without touching the source tree, read-only export destination rules, restore using the selected workspace and preserving evidence after a post-replace failure), and the desktop suites pass. Foreground visuals, real pointer/keyboard use and the export folder in Finder remain for an idle desktop.

### Recovery-water pixel gate — authorized tolerance (user decision, 2026-11-20)

- The user authorised a 「视觉不可区分」 tolerance for the recovery-water pixel comparison only. This is a scoped exception to the earlier zero-tolerance rule; every other pixel comparison in the matrix keeps zero tolerance and no other comparison may cite this decision.
- Conditions that keep the exception honest:
  1. It applies only to the recovery-water animation frames, not to any other surface, layout or state.
  2. No masks, crops or excluded regions: the whole card area at the agreed window size and DPR is compared.
  3. The comparison must be deterministic and phase-aligned: both sides are rasterised from the same source at fixed clock samples and the same logical size/scale, so any remaining difference is rasterisation (GPU/software, anti-aliasing, colour management) and not geometry, timing or content.
  4. Per-frame thresholds are evidence-based: a first measurement pass reports the distribution (percentile of per-pixel max-channel delta, mean delta, worst pixel, edge position) and the pass bars are set from that distribution, recorded with the numbers; changing a bar later requires a new recorded decision.
  5. The reference draw instructions must keep matching the native layer functions (they already do to 1e-8 per `K-recovery-water`); if they drift, the pixel comparison is invalid regardless of thresholds.
- Timing/phase alignment is part of the gate: an independently triggered live recovery is not a valid sample, because the phase differs; the gate uses fixed-clock offscreen frames.

### K-recovery-water fixes — reduced-motion resize clock (2026-11-20)

- A size change during reduced motion now restarts the previous-frame timestamp (`reset_previous`) before the next sample, matching the frozen `ResizeObserver`. The `reduced · 4000 ms · resize` sample returns to `frameScale 0.35` on both sides (delta 0) and its draw instructions stay identical; normal motion keeps its clock. Verified myself: the gate's `--verify` reproduces all seven artefacts byte-identically, desktop suites pass serially (25 lib + 19 contract), fmt/identifiers/`git diff --check` clean.
- Correction to the card-header row reported by the gate: the frozen fixture mounts a bare `.terminal-pane.split-leaf`, whose header is the 32 pt split-pane header. The reference session card overrides that pane header to 42 pt (`session-canvas.css`, `.session-card .terminal-pane-header.split-pane-header`) while the water element keeps `top: 32px`, so in the real card mount the reference water also overlaps the bottom 10 pt of a 42 pt header. Native's 42 pt header plus `RecoveryWater::TOP = 32` therefore matches the reference, consistent with the earlier recorded mount facts; no card-layout or water-top change is needed. The gate's hit rows in that band still come from the split-pane fixture and need a `.session-card` rerun before they can be read as card behaviour. Real-product pointer behaviour (clicking the reference card body under water does activate it and write an anchor, while the earlier approved native rule blocks it) is already queued as WATER-POINTER for the user's decision, so no wiring changes here.
- Content eligibility (`invisible-text`) and the handoff order remain recorded divergences for `G-recovery-water`.

### K-pty-flow — UTF-8, batching, credit, submission and replay (2026-11-20)

- 27 fixed cases: split UTF-8 (including combining marks, emoji and invalid surrogates) decodes byte-identically; 16 ms first-chunk deadlines, cap flush and batch order match; credit pause/resume, cumulative acknowledgements and over-acknowledgement errors match; CR/LF/CRLF one-write or split-write submission and bracketed paste match; journal bounds and replay envelopes match. I reproduced the gate's `--verify` (six artefacts byte-identical) and ran the contract tests (3 pass, capture ignored) plus fmt/identifiers/diff checks.
- Two recorded product gaps, not fixed here. (1) Native has no host "send text" submission entry, so the frozen two-write path (body, then `0d` after echo settles) has no counterpart; this belongs to the still-pending `mt` host-control/CLI work, while ordinary byte input is unaffected. (2) Reopening a non-Shell session does not restore the previous terminal contents: native only seeds Shell history (`owner.rs` `restored_history` is empty for ClaudeCode), so the old journal screen never enters the session and no startup resize/boundary frames are appended, while frozen replays the journal, scrolls the old text into scrollback and appends both frames. Final grid and visible viewport happen to match (empty after the boundary). Queued for `G-pty-flow`/`G-terminal-replay` with a replay design decision; real PTY load, 16 ms scheduling jitter, reconnect and Windows ConPTY remain for the G-level gates.

### Recovery-water pixel gate — measured bars (root decision, 2026-11-20)

- The offscreen measurement pass is deterministic: two full runs and an independent `--verify` reproduce 18 PNGs, the statistics, the schedule and the report byte-identically (hidden Electron 43.4.1 / Chromium 150.0.7871.224, DPR 2, light theme, water region `[0,32,560,600]` on a 560×632 session card with the 42 pt header, 9 frames from 0 to 7200 ms at 16.667 ms steps). Both sides are rasterised from the same source, same size and same phase; frozen is a real React build of the frozen component inside a hidden `.session-card`, native is the production `draw_background → draw_fluid → draw_edges` path composited with the documented premultiplied source-over formula. Water path vertices match to 5.68e-14 pt.
- Measured distribution over all 1,344,000 pixels per frame: mean 1.015–1.234, p99 ≤ 3, p99.9 ≤ 4, worst pixel 20 (only at the two bottom corners, where Chromium masks the whole card and native applies the radius per layer), >8 at most 25 pixels (0.00186%), >16 at most 2, >32 none. Column-wise first-difference of the water edge is ≤ 2 device px on every frame except the 16.667 ms weak fade-in (max 3). I looked at the 2400 ms and 144 ms pairs myself: indistinguishable at 1:1; the bulk difference is a uniform 1-level quantization/colour-management shift.
- Bars, per frame, over the whole unmasked region: mean ≤ 2.0; p99 ≤ 4; p99.9 ≤ 6; per-pixel max ≤ 32; >8 ≤ 67 px (0.005%); >16 ≤ 134 px (0.01%); >32 = 0. Structural: edge position difference p99 ≤ 3 and max ≤ 4 device px. Draw-instruction guard: path vertices ≤ 1e-8 pt and an exact frame/schedule/DPR match. Rationale: the bars sit just above the observed distribution, the max bar leaves rasterisation headroom while a systematic shift of that size would trip mean/p99, and the structural bar still catches any real edge or geometry move. Changing any bar later requires a new recorded decision.
- Scope of this gate: only these offscreen water frames. Real GPU presentation, native external shadows, window occlusion, dark theme, other DPR/scales and the live first-frame handoff remain unverified at the G-level gate and may not cite this tolerance.
