### WATER-POINTER implementation — activation under water (2026-11-20)

- Clicking a card while its recovery water covers it now behaves like a click without water: the active card switches immediately (the level focus uses the pending card), the geometry anchor is written, and `FocusSession` is sent as soon as the transport accepts commands. While a replacement runtime is not ready the card still activates locally and exactly one `FocusSession` is sent after the fresh ready graph; a repeated click or a second ready graph does not replay it. Terminal bytes remain blocked until the water clears.
- Card activation now uses one shared judgement: half-open rectangle (exact right/bottom edges outside) and 12 pt clipping on all four corners, read from the bounds measured in the same frame. The `K-recovery-water` native column changes only at `right-exact`, `bottom-exact` and `bottom-corner` (true → false); the gate's `--verify` stays byte-identical. Hidden probes cover four scenarios (uncovered body, covered overlap band `y=36`, covered body, covered reconnect), the optimistic state, single deferred send, edge/corner non-activation and input blocking. I ran the probe, the contract test, the gate and fmt/identifiers/diff-check myself.
- Real pointer behaviour, focus rings and the actual anchor persistence stay with `G-recovery-water-pointer`/REC-16 on an idle desktop.

### G-mt-control-host research adopted (root decision, 2026-11-20)

- The research lane read the frozen control plane and the 64 host-control / 46 shell-entry cases; I spot-checked its endpoint and token claims against `host-control-server.ts`. Adopted design: Unix domain socket at `<dataRoot>/control/runtime.sock` (directory `0700`, socket `0600`, stale path removed before bind), v1 4-byte-BE-length JSON frames with a 2 MiB cap, in-memory SHA-256 token digests bound to `{runId, sessionId, scopes, expiresAt, runtimeGeneration}`, the frozen rejection order and error codes, and one transport implementation per platform (Windows named pipe later). The research's alternative (loopback HTTP) is rejected for this stage: it needs port discovery and a wider network surface without matching the frozen connection behaviour as directly.
- First closure (MT-11) is read-only: `host.identify` and `host.list` only, real socket in an isolated short temp root, legal/expired/revoked/out-of-scope tokens, version/method/field/deadline errors, target guards (explicit id, ref revision, sibling/relative, missing, ambiguous), zero writes/events/PTY/focus on every rejection, endpoint permissions and rebind after shutdown. Managed Shell/Agent launches get endpoint + token; the token is revoked on exit, replacement, disposal and launch failure. Terminal input, creation, navigation, confirmations and Windows pipes stay for later gates.
- Decisions still required before the write gates (not blocking the read-only closure): which real user data the control plane may write and whether targets may cross workspaces; which processes may inherit the token and whether it should be short-lived/renewable; whether to expose a loopback port or a Windows control entry; and whether to fix the frozen managed `cc` positional-argument bug (`current` → `--canvas current`) with its visible exit-code change.

### G-mt-control-host — read-only control plane (2026-11-20)

- Implemented the first closure: the writable runtime binds `<dataRoot>/control/runtime.sock` (directory `0700`, socket `0600`) after taking the owner lease; read-only and same-process recovery startups never bind. The v1 framed protocol, token service (32-byte bearer, SHA-256 digest in memory, per-run revocation, expiry boundary), rejection order and public error envelope match the frozen fixtures for `identify`/`list`, including the projection hash. Tokens inject `MATOU_CONTROL_ENDPOINT`/`MATOU_CONTROL_TOKEN` into managed launches after clearing inherited control variables; they are revoked on worker exit, replacement, disposal, stop and launch failure. This stage deliberately grants only `host.identify`/`host.list`; terminal reads/writes, creation, navigation and confirmations stay for later gates.
- Deliberate deviations from frozen, both safety-side: the endpoint refuses a symlink, a non-socket file, a live socket it does not own, or a non-owned control directory instead of deleting it, and a failed control start is logged (path + reason) while the runtime and terminals keep running. A resident process must not delete user files or lose its terminal over an optional control plane.
- Verification I reproduced: 11 host-control tests including real Unix sockets, 156 runtime tests (0 failed, 6 pre-existing ignored) serial, 44 desktop tests serial, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo check --locked --all-targets`, identifiers and whitespace. Rejections and the positive reads compare every database table, journal bytes, owner events and the terminal spy before/after; no writes. Not verified: the real `mt` CLI, desktop focus/geometry/notification delivery, real user data, real crash injection, descendant credential inheritance, and Windows named pipes/DACL.

### G-mt-discovery — `mt --help/identify/list [--all]` (2026-11-20)

- The runtime now installs `<dataRoot>/control/bin/mt` (0700, ownership-checked) as a shim to the current runtime executable and prepends that directory to every managed launch's PATH together with the run token. A hidden `--internal-mt` dispatch runs the CLI before the stdio handshake and database open, so `mt --help` works with no identity and a missing endpoint/token exits 4 without connecting. `identify`, `list` and `list --all` match the frozen text/JSON, ordering and projection hash; target selectors are encoded client-side for later commands but are not public CLI syntax yet, matching the frozen discovery commands. Response version/ID mismatches, EOF, malformed/oversized frames and timeouts map to the frozen exit codes without retrying.
- Verification I reproduced: 14 control-plane tests, 5 CLI process tests (including a real PTY PATH check and a runtime-move shim refresh), 164 runtime tests (0 failed, 6 ignored), 44 desktop tests, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo check --locked --all-targets`, identifiers and whitespace. The lane also added the frozen missing-target/ordinal/ambiguity messages to the read-only control plane.
- Not verified: real desktop focus, a live in-use managed terminal, the installed `.app` upgrade path, Windows, real user data, and exhausting the 5 s connection budget during connect.

### Gateway stage 3 research adopted — first slice: web-search target (2026-11-20)

The six scenarios remain unverified until the user unlocks the console and the lane is re-run.

### G-codex-launch slice 1 — Shell promotion to a stub Codex (2026-11-20)

- Exact `codex` and the frozen bypass command (after trimming and clearing the typed line) now stop the Shell and start a Codex TUI on the same card and graph node, keeping cwd, view and grid; parameterized commands (`codex --model …`), `env codex`, `./codex`, `CODEX` and mixed Claude flags stay in the Shell. A missing command or an immediate exit shows the frozen copy and rolls back to a working Shell, with the old run saved as exited. Per the decision, this slice writes only `kind=codex`/`profile=codex` and no trusted provider binding; the UUID-backed binding waits for the events gate. A stub CLI (`codex-launch.sh`) supplied the first screen, argv and cwd so no real account or upstream was used.
- The combined tree exposed a pre-existing cwd-lookup bug: macOS `lsof` was resolved from the caller's PATH and ran under the caller's locale, so a restricted PATH or `LC_ALL=C` produced escaped `\xNN` paths and the session cwd never updated. Fixed by using `/usr/sbin/lsof` and running only that query under `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`; Shell/Codex child locales are unchanged. I reproduced the failing test before the fix and after it, then ran runtime 182 / core 150 / desktop 50 with 0 failures, fmt, `cargo check --locked --all-targets`, identifiers and whitespace.
- Not verified: a real Codex login/first screen/turn, trusted SessionStart and provider UUID, events, resume, permissions, title, history, HUD, Fork, mt creation, Windows and visible DAG.

### Custom compaction target — two frozen paths (2026-11-20)

- `compaction` now persists as `{mode: follow|custom, providerId?, model?, effort?}` with tri-state patching and the same atomic preference write as the tiers and web search; the new `Get/SetClaudeModelPreferences` exchange is additive while the old assignment commands keep their reply shape. Validation matches the frozen copy for missing provider/key, empty or unsupported catalog, subscription-as-API, invalid effort and bad identifiers; corrupt stored fields fall back to the row default while a later-invalid saved target keeps its value for settings and reports at launch.
- Routing follows the frozen matrix: only `POST /v1/messages` with exactly `x-claude-code-compaction: manual` or `auto` (case-sensitive value, case-insensitive header name, no duplicates/merged values) goes to the compaction target; counts ignore the marker; no marker or other values follow the search/tier route. A session with tiers or a search target resolves compaction in the launch snapshot (known window <100K or an unavailable target fails startup; non-follow `claude-*` gets adaptive thinking, others drop thinking, `max_tokens` follows the target). The custom-only legacy path keeps the frozen lazy behaviour: startup succeeds, the first marked request resolves and caches the target, failures are request-level 502 and retryable. Failures never fall back to the working model; DeepSeek targets still run the inline-tool conversion.
- Verification: the gate records the 15-row marker matrix, preference/validation cases, snapshot cases (new, alive, cold resume, fork, permission restart, unknown window, 99999 window), generation fields/errors and DeepSeek combinations; runtime 182 / core 150 / desktop 50 all pass after the cwd fix. Desktop UI for the compaction row, real CLI compaction timing/results, subscriptions/OAuth and Windows remain unverified.

### IME real check — three attempts stopped before the first key (2026-11-20)

- Attempt 1 saw the isolated window's title match but `frontmost=false` at the first-key guard; attempt 2 activated by PID and read back true three times, then saw false again at the first key; attempt 3 began after macOS had locked the console (21:54:06) and every activation returned `NO_WINDOW`. Zero keystrokes were sent, the PTY received zero input bytes, the input source stayed Sogou, and the isolated instance was shut down with no leftovers; the frozen side was never started. The next attempt should run after an unlock, activate by PID and send each scenario's keys in one AppleScript immediately after the guard, and keep `screencapture` outside the key sequence.

### G-codex-events slice 1 — lifecycle, trusted identity and notifications (2026-11-20)

- A Codex card now follows the current turn after a trusted `SessionStart`: the rollout path must be absolute, canonical, inside this run's `CODEX_HOME`, a `.jsonl` and carry the hook's UUID; only then is the binding written (the gap left by the launch slice). Bytes alone, an old token, another run or a duplicate hook cannot bind. States move idle→running→needs-input→running→idle, approvals and normal completions each emit their notification, an in-turn error emits one error and suppresses the following Stop completion, and the visible Esc notice is consumed once. Tool/turn duplicates are deduplicated, and a Canvas switch keeps a late notification on its original card.
- The independent DAG window is out of scope here because `G-dag-window` has not delivered it; this slice verifies the live graph snapshot, notifications and focus switching, and the window refresh is deferred to that gate by decision. Verification I reproduced: 4 Codex event process tests, runtime 186 / core 150 / desktop 50 with 0 failures, fmt, `cargo check --locked --all-targets`, identifiers and whitespace. Not verified: a real Codex login/turn, real approvals/Esc, the standalone DAG window, resume/permission/title/history/HUD/Fork/mt and Windows.

### G-codex-permission slice 1 — four modes, restart and menu (2026-11-20)

Switching a card stops the old run, resumes the same UUID, and only counts the switch after the new `SessionStart` confirms that UUID; until then the menu keeps the old label and disables further switching, and a timeout goes to the retryable failure card with the chosen mode preserved. Invalid stored modes fall back to Default with the frozen warning and update the binding on confirmation; a newly invalid switch reports an error and keeps the current process. ### Recovery water — real crash, input gate and handoff (2026-11-21)

- Single card passed in a real window: after SIGKILLing the isolated runtime, the water is first sampled at +5.2–12.9 ms (gradient by +26.9 ms), a real `x` during the water reaches the PTY with 0 bytes, and the water removal coincides with the six restored history lines in the same sampled frame; the new PTY's prompt appears ~68 ms later, the same card/focus/geometry/history survive, and a later `x` delivers `78` without re-clicking. The reference clone also removed the water on its first restored-text frame and delivered 0 bytes during the water, but its display showed a phantom `x` after receding that had no PTY byte (a reference-side display quirk native does not share); its new prompt arrived ~42 ms after receding.
- Two-card run: killing the shared runtime waters both cards and interrupts both PTYs. The gate's “only the left card is recovering” assumption was **not** compared against the reference's two-card behaviour, and the frozen clone also uses one utility runtime; this is recorded as an unverified architecture question, not a confirmed product difference. A first cut, if the reference is proven to isolate per session, is an independent per-session worker failure/recovery while a whole-runtime exit still waters every affected card.
- Recorded alias bug: launching the isolated data root through a `/.vol/<dev>/<ino>` same-inode alias made the replacement runtime enter `takeover-sidecar-unusable` and never start a PTY, while direct paths recover. Minimal fix: normalize the takeover lock's parent to the real mount path while keeping NOFOLLOW on the final component, plus a same-inode alias takeover test. Real GPU presentation, external shadow, dark theme, other scales, hidden scroll position and IME during water remain unverified.
